```
1	# Notes
2	
3	This directory contains various notes and documentation related to the project.
4	Each file is organized by topic for easy reference.
5	
6	By default there are chores-*.md and todo.md. Chores are general notes
7	about tasks and todo.md contains short term tasks and their status.
8	
9	In the future we I expect we may want to create a "notes"
10	database to better manage the information, TBD.
11	
12	Examples chore file:
13	```
14	# Chores-01.md
15	 
16	General maintenance tasks and considerations for the project see other files for
17	more specific topics. A chore in a chores file provides quick information on the
18	how and why of a particular chore.
19	
20	## Create a binary that lists jj info 
21	
22	This binary should list the changeID, commitID, and description title
23	and using `jj-lib`
24	```
25	
26	## Workflow and conventions
27	
28	Bot-facing workflow, versioning, and code conventions live in
29	[`../CLAUDE.md`](../CLAUDE.md). Start there for:
30	
31	- **Versioning during development** — single-step vs multi-step,
32	  `-N` pre-release suffixes, done-marker discipline.
33	- **Code Conventions** — doc comments on every file / fn / method,
34	  `// OK: …` justifications on `unwrap*` calls, ask-on-ambiguity,
35	  stuck detection.
36	- **Commit-Push-Finalize Flow** — two-checkpoint per-step
37	  discipline with hard stop after finalize.
38	
39	## Todo format
40	
41	Todo.md contains two main sections "Todo" and "Done" each item is a
42	short explanations of a tasks and links to more details using 1 or more
43	references.
44	
45	Multiple references must be separated: `[2],[3]` not `[2,3]` or `[2][3]`.
46	In markdown, `[2,3]` is a single ref key (won't resolve) and `[2][3]`
47	is parsed as display text `2` with ref key `3` (so `[2]` won't resolve).
48	
49	Examples:
50	
51	# Todo
52	- Add new feature X [details](features.md#feature-x)
53	- Fix bug Y [1]
54	
55	# Done
56	- Fixed issue Z [2],[3]
57	
58	[1]: bugs.md#bug-y
59	[2]: issues.md#issue-z
60	[3]: fixes.md#fix-z
61	
```

920	/// stripped. Empty input aborts the push.
921	fn compose_message_via_editor(
922	    layout: &StateLayout,
923	) -> Result<(String, String), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
924	    let editor = std::env::var("EDITOR")
925	        .or_else(|_| std::env::var("VISUAL"))
926	        .unwrap_or_else(|_| "vi".to_string()); // OK: POSIX fallback when nothing is configured
927	    let msg_path = layout
928	        .path
929	        .parent()
930	        .ok_or("push:message: state layout has no parent dir")?
931	        .join("push-message.txt");
932	    if let Some(parent) = msg_path.parent() {
933	        fs::create_dir_all(parent)?;
934	    }
935	    let template = "\
936	# Write the commit title on the first line (target ~50 chars).
937	# Lines starting with `#` are ignored.
938	# Leave everything blank to abort the push.
939	#
940	# Body goes after a blank line. ochid trailers are appended
941	# per-repo automatically — don't add them here.
942	";
943	    fs::write(&msg_path, template)?;
944	    info!("push:message: launching {editor} on {}", msg_path.display());
945	    let status = std::process::Command::new(&editor)
946	        .arg(&msg_path)
947	        .status()
948	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to launch {editor}: {e}"))?;
949	    if !status.success() {
950	        return Err(format!("{editor} exited non-zero ({status})").into());
951	    }
952	    let content = fs::read_to_string(&msg_path)?;
953	    let _ = fs::remove_file(&msg_path);
954	    parse_message(&content)
955	        .ok_or_else(|| "push:message: template was left empty or all-comments — aborting".into())
956	}
957	
958	/// Parse the editor-saved message into `(title, body)`. Strips
959	/// `#`-prefixed comment lines, trims surrounding blanks, and
960	/// splits on the first blank line after the title. Returns `None`
961	/// when the message has no non-comment content.
962	fn parse_message(raw: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {
963	    let mut meaningful = String::new();
964	    for line in raw.lines() {
965	        let trimmed_left = line.trim_start();
966	        if trimmed_left.starts_with('#') {
967	            continue;
968	        }
969	        meaningful.push_str(line);
970	        meaningful.push('\n');
971	    }
972	    let trimmed = meaningful.trim();
973	    if trimmed.is_empty() {
974	        return None;
975	    }
976	    let mut parts = trimmed.splitn(2, "\n\n");
977	    let title = parts.next().unwrap_or("").trim().to_string(); // OK: splitn always yields ≥1
978	    let body = parts.next().unwrap_or("").trim().to_string(); // OK: empty body if no blank line
979	    if title.is_empty() {
980	        return None;
981	    }
982	    Some((title, body))
983	}

> AGENT

Both go in **one editor session**. The template (`push-message.txt`) opens with this header:

```
# Write the commit title on the first line (target ~50 chars).
# Lines starting with `#` are ignored.
# Leave everything blank to abort the push.
#
# Body goes after a blank line. ochid trailers are appended
# per-repo automatically — don't add them here.
```

The expected shape is:

```
Your title here

Your body paragraph here.
- bullet
- bullet
```

`#` lines are stripped, then the content is split on the **first blank line** — everything before is the title, everything after is the body (`src/push.rs:962-983`). Save once with `:wq` and you're done; there is no second prompt.

So in your case, with only a title typed and no blank-line+body, `parse_message` would have set `title = <what you typed>` and `body = ""`. The push wouldn't have prompted again — it would have proceeded with an empty body to the message-approval gate.

If you'd like, the help text in `vc-x1 push --help` / the editor template could be made more explicit ("write title, blank line, then body — all in this one file"), since the current wording assumes you read it carefully. Want me to tighten the template wording?

> DEVELOPER

optionaly by Body on subsequent lines.
A blank line will be inserted automatically

```
  parses; `BANNER` emitted as `info!` at the start of every
  run except the detached `finalize --exec` re-entry, and
  suppressed when the active subcommand has `--no-label` set
  (so `chid -L` / `desc -L` / `list -L` / `show -L` stay
  script-parseable)

#### 0.37.0-5 — polish

- `src/push.rs` — `--dry-run`: skip every side-effect subprocess
  (preflight / commit-app / commit-claude / bookmark-both /
  push-app / finalize-claude), emit `[dry-run] would run: ...`
  lines instead; review still shows the diff (that's the *point*
  of dry-run); state file not persisted in dry-run so a later
  real run starts clean. `--step`: after each completed stage
  (with a next stage pending), prompt `[y/N]` to continue;
  `--yes` short-circuits; non-tty without `--yes` errors fast.
  Non-tty detection via `std::io::IsTerminal` — review stage
  errors when stdin isn't a tty and `--yes` isn't set; message
  stage does the same before launching `$EDITOR`.
  `check_gitignore_coherence` — reads `.gitignore` at the
  workspace root and warns (non-fatal) when the configured
  state-dir name isn't present. Helps catch the "user changed
  `[push].state-dir` in `.vc-config.toml` and forgot to update
  `.gitignore`" case.

#### 0.37.0 — docs + workflow migration (done marker)

- `src/push.rs` — fold `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` into `preflight`
  as the first step (divergence resolved before cargo burns
  cycles); matches the 0.36.3 design's "push preflight calls
  sync" note.
- `CLAUDE.md` (both repos, byte-identical) — rewrite
  Commit-Push-Finalize Flow around `vc-x1 push`: intro says
  "use push"; drops the two-checkpoint manual ceremony
  (Checkpoint 1 / Checkpoint 2 / Finalize the .claude repo);
  keeps "After finalize: stop and wait" and "Late changes
  after push"; adds a "Manual finalize fallback" subsection
  for `--no-finalize` and post-failure cases; pre-step sync
  section reframed as "still useful" (push runs sync internally
  but running it earlier surfaces divergence sooner).
- `README.md` — new `### push` subsection under Usage: stage
  table, flag table, state-file config, link to this chore's
  per-step record.
- `notes/chores-05.md` — per-step record filled in for this
  step; block now complete through `0.37.0`.

**Status: shipped.** The push subcommand design is implemented,
tested, and documented. The manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow is
retired in CLAUDE.md — `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` is the primary
entry point going forward.

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/push.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Parse the editor-saved message into `(title, body)`. Strips\n/// `#`-prefixed comment lines, trims surrounding blanks, and\n/// splits on the first blank line after the title. Returns `None`\n/// when the message has no non-comment content.\nfn parse_message(raw: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {\n    let mut meaningful = String::new();\n    for line in raw.lines() {\n        let trimmed_left = line.trim_start();\n        if trimmed_left.starts_with('#') {\n            continue;\n        }\n        meaningful.push_str(line);\n        meaningful.push('\\n');\n    }\n    let trimmed = meaningful.trim();\n    if trimmed.is_empty() {\n        return None;\n    }\n    let mut parts = trimmed.splitn(2, \"\\n\\n\");\n    let title = parts.next().unwrap_or(\"\").trim().to_string(); // OK: splitn always yields ≥1\n    let body = parts.next().unwrap_or(\"\").trim().to_string(); // OK: empty body if no blank line\n    if title.is_empty() {\n        return None;\n    }\n    Some((title, body))\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Parse the editor-saved message into `(title, body)`. Strips\n/// `#`-prefixed comment lines, trims surrounding blanks, and treats\n/// the first non-comment line as the title with everything after as\n/// the body (a blank line between is allowed but not required —\n/// the commit stages insert the title/body separator themselves).\n/// Returns `None` when the message has no non-comment content.\nfn parse_message(raw: &str) -> Option<(String, String)> {\n    let mut meaningful = String::new();\n    for line in raw.lines() {\n        let trimmed_left = line.trim_start();\n        if trimmed_left.starts_with('#') {\n            continue;\n        }\n        meaningful.push_str(line);\n        meaningful.push('\\n');\n    }\n    let trimmed = meaningful.trim();\n    if trimmed.is_empty() {\n        return None;\n    }\n    let mut lines = trimmed.lines();\n    let title = lines.next().unwrap_or(\"\").trim().to_string(); // OK: trimmed non-empty ⇒ first line exists\n    let body = lines.collect::<Vec<_>>().join(\"\\n\").trim().to_string();\n    if title.is_empty() {\n        return None;\n    }\n    Some((title, body))\n}"
}

{

  "old_string": "    /// `parse_message` extracts title + body, strips `#` comments,\n    /// and rejects all-comments / empty input.\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_message_cases() {\n        // Title + body separated by blank line.\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: x\\n\\nBody here.\\nSecond line.\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: x\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"Body here.\\nSecond line.\");\n\n        // Comments stripped.\n        let (t, b) =\n            parse_message(\"# comment\\nfeat: y\\n# mid-comment\\n\\nbody\\n# tail-comment\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: y\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"body\");\n\n        // Title only (no body).\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: z\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: z\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"\");\n\n        // All comments → None (caller aborts).\n        assert!(parse_message(\"# only comments\\n# and more\\n\").is_none());\n        // All blank → None.\n        assert!(parse_message(\"   \\n\\n\").is_none());\n    }",
  "new_string": "    /// `parse_message` extracts title + body, strips `#` comments,\n    /// and rejects all-comments / empty input.\n    #[test]\n    fn parse_message_cases() {\n        // Title + body separated by blank line.\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: x\\n\\nBody here.\\nSecond line.\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: x\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"Body here.\\nSecond line.\");\n\n        // Title + body with no blank line — first line is title, rest is body.\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: x\\nBody here.\\nSecond line.\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: x\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"Body here.\\nSecond line.\");\n\n        // Body with internal blank lines preserved.\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: x\\npara 1\\n\\npara 2\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: x\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"para 1\\n\\npara 2\");\n\n        // Comments stripped.\n        let (t, b) =\n            parse_message(\"# comment\\nfeat: y\\n# mid-comment\\n\\nbody\\n# tail-comment\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: y\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"body\");\n\n        // Title only (no body).\n        let (t, b) = parse_message(\"feat: z\\n\").unwrap();\n        assert_eq!(t, \"feat: z\");\n        assert_eq!(b, \"\");\n\n        // All comments → None (caller aborts).\n        assert!(parse_message(\"# only comments\\n# and more\\n\").is_none());\n        // All blank → None.\n        assert!(parse_message(\"   \\n\\n\").is_none());\n    }"
}

The manual Commit-Push-Finalize Flow is\nretired in CLAUDE.md — `vc-x1 push <bookmark>` is the primary\nentry point going forward.\n\n## Tighten `$EDITOR` template wording (0.37.1)\n\nFirst dogfood of the interactive `vc-x1 push` flow exposed two\npapercuts in the editor template:\n\n1. The header was structured \"title on first line / blank line /\n   body after\" — easy to read as \"title now, body in a second\n   prompt later\". A user typed only the title, `:wq`'d, and was\n   surprised when push proceeded with an empty body instead of\n   re-prompting.\n2. If a user typed title-then-body with no blank between,\n   `parse_message`'s `splitn(2, \"\\n\\n\")` made the *whole thing*\n   the title and the body empty — silent footgun.\n\nThe fix is to make the template's contract explicit and to relax\nthe parser so the blank-line separator is not user-required.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — template header rewritten:\n\n  > Leave as-is to abort. > AGENT

All green. Here's the summary of what changed:

**Direct answer to your question:** the `$EDITOR` opens **one file** with both title and body in the same session. The old template said "Body goes after a blank line" — easy to misread as "you'll get a second prompt". You did the right thing for the wrong contract.

**Changes for 0.37.1** (single-step, post-ship polish on the `0.37.0` push arc):

- `src/push.rs` — template header rewritten with your wording: contract stated up front, both auto-insertions (separator blank line + ochid trailer) called out so the user knows not to type either.
- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message` relaxed: first non-comment line = title, remainder = body. Blank line between is now allowed but not required (the commit-stage `format!` calls already insert the separator). This closes the silent footgun where typing title-then-body with no blank between made the *whole thing* the title.
- `src/push.rs` — two new `parse_message_cases` tests: no-blank-line case, multi-paragraph body with internal blank lines.

554	### test-fixture
555	
556	Scaffold a throwaway dual-repo jj workspace + local bare-git remotes
557	for testing `finalize` (and other subcommands) without touching live
558	workspace repos. Mirrors the real `vc-x1 init` layout minus the GitHub
559	side and the `~/.claude/projects/` symlink. Both repos get a described
560	initial commit with matching `ochid:` trailers, a tracked `main`
561	bookmark, and a pushed remote — so `finalize --push` flows work
562	end-to-end on either side.
563	
564	**Local remotes, not GitHub.** Each `origin` points at a bare-git
565	directory alongside the work trees (`<base>/remote-code.git/`,
566	`<base>/remote-claude.git/`) — no network, no auth, no GitHub. Pushes
567	succeed against these local bare repos and stay inside the fixture,
568	so nothing leaks out and nothing needs cleanup on a remote service.
569	When you're done, `vc-x1 test-fixture-rm <base>` wipes the whole
570	thing.
571	
572	```bash
573	vc-x1 test-fixture                 # base = $TMPDIR/vc-x1-test-<timestamp>
574	vc-x1 test-fixture --path /tmp/t1  # explicit path
575	vc-x1 test-fixture --use-template ../vc-template-x1  # seed from sibling templates
576	```
577	
578	`--use-template` takes the same `CODE[,BOT]` value as `vc-x1 init`
579	(bot defaults to `<CODE>.claude` sibling). Non-hidden template contents
580	are copied into `work/` and `work/.claude/`, and each repo's
581	`README.md` first line is rewritten to `# work` / `# work.claude`.
582	This is the path for eyeballing the template-copy result without
583	hitting GitHub.
584	
585	Layout:
586	```
587	<base>/
588	  remote-code.git/     bare git remote for code repo
589	  remote-claude.git/   bare git remote for .claude session repo
590	  work/                code repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
591	    .vc-config.toml    path="/",       other-repo=".claude"
592	    .gitignore         /.claude /.git /.jj /target
593	    .claude/           session repo (jj colocated, main tracks origin)
594	      .vc-config.toml  path="/.claude", other-repo=".."
595	      .gitignore       .git .jj
596	```
597	
598	Example — running `vc-x1 test-fixture` (the timestamp suffix varies):
599	```

635	  vc-x1 test-fixture-rm /tmp/vc-x1-test-8PD4x8
636	```
637	
638	### Testing push + finalize
639	
640	Always test against a throwaway fixture, never the live workspace.
641	Scaffold one with `test-fixture` (above), then run the complete
642	push + finalize flow end-to-end. The code repo uses plain
643	`jj git push`; the session repo uses `vc-x1 finalize` to squash
644	trailing writes and push in one shot.
645	
646	```bash
647	base=$(mktemp -u /tmp/vc-x1-test-XXXXXX)
648	vc-x1 test-fixture --path "$base"
649	work="$base/work"
650	session="$base/work/.claude"
651	
652	# 1. code repo: described commit → advance main → push
653	echo hello > "$work/hello.txt"
654	jj describe @ -R "$work" -m 'feat: add hello.txt'
655	jj bookmark set main -r @ -R "$work"
656	jj git push -R "$work"
657	
658	# 2. session repo: trailing writes → finalize (squash into @-, push)
659	echo notes > "$session/notes.md"
660	vc-x1 finalize --repo "$session" --squash --push main --detach \
661	    --log "$session/finalize.log"
662	
663	# 3. inspect the detached child's log once it's done (≈10s by default)
664	sleep 12 && cat "$session/finalize.log"
665	
666	# 4. cleanup when done
667	vc-x1 test-fixture-rm "$base"
668	```
669	
670	**Why `jj git push` for code but `finalize` for `.claude`?** The
671	code repo's workflow is a plain dev commit on `@-` that we push
672	directly. The session repo mirrors the bot's runtime pattern:
673	session writes land in `@` (above the last committed dev commit),
```

> AGENT

Yes — your assumption is right. Concrete recipe:

```bash
# 1. Scaffold the fixture (use --path or let it pick a tmp path)
vc-x1 test-fixture --path ./tf-1
cd ./tf-1/work

# 2. Make a change in the code repo
echo hello > hello.txt

# 3. Optional but realistic — also touch the session repo so the
#    .claude side has something to commit (the fixture has matching
#    main bookmarks + remotes on both sides)
echo session > .claude/session.txt

# 4. Run interactive push
vc-x1 push main

# 5. When done
cd /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1   # or wherever
vc-x1 test-fixture-rm ./tf-1         # path is relative to whichever cwd you run it from
```

What you'll see:

1. **Preflight** — sync + fmt + clippy + test. ⚠️ The fixture has no `Cargo.toml` so cargo steps won't fire as in real use; preflight will exit early on whichever check it can't run. (The integration tests pass `--no-finalize` and skip the cargo bits via test plumbing — see `src/push.rs:1660`.) You may need to use `--from review` or `--from message` to skip past preflight when dogfooding the editor flow specifically. Worth verifying.
2. **Review gate** — diff stats printed, `[y/N]` prompt.
3. **Message gate** — `$EDITOR` opens on `tf-1/work/.vc-x1/push-message.txt`. Test: type only a title and `:wq` → should now produce title with empty body (used to produce title-with-stuck-body); type title + body with no blank line → should split correctly (the bug fix).
4. **Gitignore warning** — fixture's `.gitignore` doesn't list `.vc-x1`, so a non-fatal warning will fire. Expected; ignore it.

There **isn't** a documented `vc-x1 push` test recipe — README §"Testing push + finalize" still uses the pre-`push` manual flow (`jj git push` + `vc-x1 finalize`). Worth adding a §"Testing push interactively" subsection if you find this recipe useful — say the word and I'll fold it in (still as part of 0.37.1, since it's directly motivated by the same dogfood that surfaced the template wording).

One thing to verify before you run: whether preflight's cargo steps are skippable on a fixture without a Cargo.toml, or whether they'll block the test. Want me to check first?

486	/// Log a warning when the configured state path isn't matched by a
487	/// `.gitignore` entry. The check is intentionally simple — looks
488	/// for `<state_dir>` or `/<state_dir>` as a line in `.gitignore`
489	/// at the repo root. Misses trickier patterns (nested wildcards,
490	/// ignore files further down the tree) but catches the common
491	/// "user changed the config and forgot to update .gitignore"
492	/// case, which is the whole point.
493	fn check_gitignore_coherence(root: &Path, state_dir_name: &str) {
494	    let gitignore = root.join(".gitignore");
495	    let matched = match fs::read_to_string(&gitignore) {
496	        Ok(content) => content.lines().any(|line| {
497	            let l = line.trim();
498	            l == state_dir_name
499	                || l == format!("/{state_dir_name}")
500	                || l == format!("{state_dir_name}/")
501	                || l == format!("/{state_dir_name}/")
502	        }),
503	        Err(_) => false,
504	    };
505	    if !matched {
506	        warn!(
507	            "push: state dir '{state_dir_name}' is not in {} — \
508	             add a '/{state_dir_name}' line so in-progress push-state \
509	             files don't get committed",
510	            gitignore.display()
511	        );
512	    }
513	}

> AGENT

Yes, agree. Here's what I propose folding into 0.37.1 (still pre-commit, so no version churn):

**1. Make the check fatal in `push`** (`src/push.rs:493`) — `check_gitignore_coherence` returns `Result<()>` instead of `warn!`. Message becomes:

```
error: push: state dir '.vc-x1' is not in <path>/.gitignore — add a '/.vc-x1' line so in-progress push-state files don't get committed
```

No bypass flag for now; fix is one line. (Per CLAUDE.md, don't design for hypothetical edge cases.)

**2. Auto-write `/.vc-x1`** in both:
- `src/init.rs:271` `GITIGNORE_CODE`
- `src/test_fixture.rs:66` `GITIGNORE_CODE`

Only the **code** repo needs it; `.claude` doesn't get a state dir from push (state lives at the workspace root, which is the app repo).

**3. Tests + docs:**
- `init.rs` and `test_fixture.rs` gitignore-content tests grow a `/.vc-x1` assertion.
- `README.md:592` fixture-layout snippet adds the `/.vc-x1` line.
- `notes/chores-05.md` 0.37.1 entry expanded to cover both fixes (template + gitignore).
- `notes/todo.md` Done line text broadened.

**Test impact:** push integration tests use `Fixture::new` → `test_fixture::test_fixture`, so once the fixture template gains the line, they keep passing under the now-fatal check.

OK to proceed?

774	/// Preflight: `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run && cargo fmt && cargo clippy
775	/// -D warnings && cargo test`.
776	///
777	/// Sync goes first so divergence with the remote is resolved before
778	/// we burn cargo cycles; if sync fails (e.g. conflicted rebase) the
779	/// build+test never runs. The cargo steps match CLAUDE.md's
780	/// pre-commit checklist (minus `cargo install` / retest, which are
781	/// project-specific). All subprocesses run in the workspace root so
782	/// cargo picks up the right `Cargo.toml`. Skipped in `--dry-run`
783	/// since `cargo fmt` writes files and sync mutates jj state.
784	fn stage_preflight(root: &Path, args: &PushArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
785	    if args.dry_run {
786	        info!(
787	            "push:preflight: [dry-run] would run vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run / cargo fmt / clippy / test"
788	        );
789	        return Ok(());
790	    }
791	    info!("push:preflight: vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run");
792	    run("vc-x1", &["sync", "--no-dry-run"], root)?;
793	    info!("push:preflight: cargo fmt");
794	    run("cargo", &["fmt"], root)?;
795	    info!("push:preflight: cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings");
796	    run(
797	        "cargo",
798	        &["clippy", "--all-targets", "--", "-D", "warnings"],
799	        root,
800	    )?;
801	    info!("push:preflight: cargo test");
802	    run("cargo", &["test"], root)?;
803	    Ok(())
804	}

```
94	/// When `--quiet` is set, the global log filter is temporarily clamped
95	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so
96	/// `info!` calls throughout sync (plus any subprocess-stderr routed
97	/// through `common::run`) go dark. Errors still surface at `Warn` /
98	/// `Error` so script callers don't lose diagnostics.
99	pub fn sync(args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
100	    let repos = resolve_repos(&args.repos);
101	    if args.quiet {
102	        let prev = log::max_level();
103	        log::set_max_level(LevelFilter::Warn);
104	        let result = sync_repos(&repos, args);
105	        log::set_max_level(prev);
106	        result
107	    } else {
108	        sync_repos(&repos, args)
109	    }
110	}
111	
112	/// Sync the given repos against their remotes.
113	///
114	/// Orchestrates the full flow: pre-flight clean-check on every repo,
115	/// snapshot each repo's current op id, then hand off to `run_plan` for
116	/// fetch + classify + (optional) act. On any error, revert every repo
117	/// to its snapshot op via `jj op restore` so the caller sees an atomic
118	/// "either it all went through or nothing changed" outcome.
119	///
120	/// Paths may be relative (resolved against the process cwd) or
121	/// absolute. Tests use absolute tempdir paths to avoid cwd dependence
122	/// under parallel `cargo test`.
123	pub fn sync_repos(repos: &[PathBuf], args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
124	    debug!(
125	        "sync: enter (no_dry_run={}, bookmark={}, remote={})",
126	        args.no_dry_run, args.bookmark, args.remote
127	    );
128	
129	    let mut snapshots: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
130	    for repo in repos {
131	        let op_id = current_op_id(repo)?;
132	        debug!("{}: op snapshot = {op_id}", repo.display());
133	        snapshots.push((repo.clone(), op_id));
134	    }
135	
136	    let result = run_plan(&snapshots, args);
137	
138	    if let Err(e) = &result {
139	        warn!("sync failed: {e}");
140	        warn!("reverting all repos to starting state...");
141	        for (repo, op_id) in &snapshots {
142	            match op_restore(repo, op_id) {
143	                Ok(()) => info!("  {}: reverted to op {op_id}", repo.display()),
144	                Err(re) => warn!("  {}: revert failed: {re}", repo.display()),
145	            }
146	        }
147	    }
148	
149	    debug!("sync: exit");
150	    result
151	}
152	
153	/// Fetch and classify each repo, then act (or not, in dry-run).
154	///
155	/// Returns `Err` on the first failure so the caller can trigger the
156	/// cross-repo revert; partial success across repos is *not* a valid
157	/// end state for this command.
158	fn run_plan(
159	    snapshots: &[(PathBuf, String)],
160	    args: &SyncArgs,
161	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
162	    // Phase 1 — fetch + classify silently.
163	    //
164	    // Each repo's fetch stderr is captured (rather than streamed to
165	    // `info!` via `common::run`) so we can decide after classification
166	    // whether to surface it. `jj git fetch`'s routine "Nothing
167	    // changed." chatter is the main thing we're suppressing here —
168	    // if nothing needs action, the user shouldn't see it.
169	    let mut fetched: Vec<(PathBuf, String)> = Vec::new();
170	    let mut ctxs: Vec<RepoCtx> = Vec::new();
171	    for (repo, op_id) in snapshots {
172	        let stderr = fetch_silent(repo, &args.remote)?;
173	        fetched.push((repo.clone(), stderr));
174	        let state = classify(repo, &args.bookmark, &args.remote)?;
175	        ctxs.push(RepoCtx {
176	            path: repo.clone(),
177	            op_id: op_id.clone(),
178	            state,
179	        });
180	    }
181	
182	    let any_action_needed = ctxs
183	        .iter()
```

214	        info!("dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply");
215	    }
216	    Ok(())
217	}
218	
219	/// Fetch `repo` from `remote` without streaming subprocess output to
220	/// `info!`.
221	///
222	/// Mirrors what `common::run` would do, but returns stderr to the
223	/// caller so the caller can decide whether to surface it (verbose /
224	/// action case) or drop it (clean case). Stdout is dropped — `jj git
225	/// fetch` doesn't use it. Failure carries stderr in the error message.
226	fn fetch_silent(repo: &Path, remote: &str) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
227	    debug!("$ jj git fetch --remote {remote} -R {}", repo.display());
228	    let output = Command::new("jj")
229	        .args(["git", "fetch", "--remote", remote, "-R", &repo_str(repo)])
230	        .output()
231	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run jj git fetch: {e}"))?;
232	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
233	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
234	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
235	        debug!("  {stdout}");
236	    }
237	    if !output.status.success() {
238	        return Err(format!("jj git fetch -R {} failed: {stderr}", repo.display()).into());
239	    }
240	    Ok(stderr)
241	}
242	
243	/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.
244	///
245	/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote
246	/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still
247	/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is
248	/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl
249	/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a
250	/// stale branch when the remote advanced.
251	///
```

> AGENT

Strong agree. `sync` mutating jj state inside preflight, before the user has even seen the review gate, is exactly the kind of surprise we should avoid.

Implementation question: dry-run sync currently prints status and returns `Ok(())` regardless of state — there's no exit signal saying "action needed." So I'd add a small flag:

**`vc-x1 sync --check`** — implies dry-run + treats `Behind`/`Diverged` as fatal. Conflicts with `--no-dry-run`. Push's preflight calls `vc-x1 sync --check`. Error message points the user at the manual fix:

```
error: sync: 1 repo needs action (see above) — resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` and re-run push
```

Output ordering preserved: per-repo state lines from `log_state` print first (so the user sees *which* repo + state), then the error.

Naming alternatives: `--strict`, `--fail-if-action`. I prefer `--check` — short, reads naturally ("just check, don't act, fail if not clean"), matches the common CLI convention (`cargo check`, `gofmt -l` style).

So 0.37.1 grows to four changes, all from the same first-dogfood session:

1. Template wording
2. Parser relax
3. Gitignore: fatal in push, auto-write in init + test-fixture
4. Sync preflight: dry-run + fatal via new `vc-x1 sync --check`

I think keeping them as one 0.37.1 commit is right — chores entry framing ("first dogfood polish") covers all of them and the diff stays scannable. Alternative: split into 0.37.1 (template+parser, already done) and 0.37.2 (gitignore+sync) so the editor fix isn't held up by the bigger change.

Your call on bundle vs split. Then I proceed with both #3 and #4.

> AGENT

Strong agree on the philosophy — explicit-in-scripts is a discipline that pays off when defaults shift. CLAUDE.md should use the explicit forms too since the bot is automation.

To confirm the naming/scope before I code:

**`src/sync.rs`** — replace the `--dry-run` / `--no-dry-run` pair with `--check` / `--no-check`. Default = `--check`. Semantics:
- `--check` (default): fetch, classify, print state, **error** if any repo is `Behind`/`Diverged`. Non-zero exit on action-needed.
- `--no-check`: fetch, classify, print, **apply** (rebase / fast-forward).

This is a breaking CLI change — `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` stops working. Since this is the user's tool and the change is small, I think replace > alias-with-deprecation. Worth flagging though.

**`src/push.rs`** preflight — explicit `vc-x1 sync --check` (not relying on default, per your "bots use explicit flags" rule).

**Docs** to update:
- `CLAUDE.md` (both copies — app and `.claude` per your dual-repo workflow): "Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync`" section + every `--no-dry-run` mention.
- `README.md`: sync usage examples.
- `notes/chores-05.md`: extend the 0.37.1 entry.
- `notes/todo.md`: done-line text.

**Tests**: sync's tests reference `no_dry_run` field — rename through. Push tests + integration tests don't hit sync directly (they use `--from message`), so no impact there.

One alternative naming I'll mention but not push: `--check` / `--apply`. Reads more symmetric and `--apply` states the positive action. But `--check` / `--no-check` matches the existing clap negation-pair convention in this codebase (you've used `--no-finalize`, `--no-label`, etc.), so consistency-wise yours wins. Sticking with your call unless you say otherwise.

Green light?

> DEVELOPER

--no-check fails on "all" errors?

> AGENT

Same errors it fails on today — `--no-check` only flips the "action needed → error" gate off. Other failures still error:

- **Fetch failure** (network, auth, ref doesn't exist) → fatal, op-restored.
- **Rebase conflict** during apply → fatal, op-restored.
- **`jj` subprocess errors** of any kind → fatal, op-restored.

The `--check` vs `--no-check` axis only controls one thing: *is "this repo needs work" itself an error?* `--check` says yes (so the user has to consciously apply); `--no-check` says no (because applying is the whole point).

So the contract is:
- `--check`: clean → exit 0; action-needed → exit 1; anything actually broken → exit 1.
- `--no-check`: clean → exit 0; action-needed → apply (then exit 0 if successful); anything actually broken (incl. failed apply) → exit 1.

160	/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.
161	///
162	/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:
163	/// - **stderr at `info!`** on success — jj prints human-readable messages
164	///   (`Moved 1 bookmarks to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) to
165	///   stderr, and the user needs to see them without requiring `-v`.
166	/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data
167	///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), so `info!` would flood the user
168	///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.
169	/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's
170	///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.
171	pub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
172	    let args_str = args.join(" ");
173	    debug!("$ {cmd} {args_str}");
174	    trace!("cwd: {}", cwd.display());
175	    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)
176	        .args(args)
177	        .current_dir(cwd)
178	        .output()
179	        .map_err(|e| format!("failed to run {cmd}: {e}"))?;
180	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();
181	    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();
182	    if !stdout.is_empty() {
183	        debug!("  {stdout}");
184	    }
185	    if !output.status.success() {
186	        return Err(format!("{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}").into());
187	    }
188	    if !stderr.is_empty() {
189	        info!("{stderr}");
190	    }
191	    Ok(stdout)
192	}
193	
194	/// Create a directory (and parents). Logs at debug level.

> AGENT

Yes — doable cleanly. Subprocess output flows through `common::run` (one helper), and that's the single point to add indentation. Right now stderr (where jj prints `Working copy now at...` etc.) goes through `info!("{stderr}")` un-indented — a one-line change to per-line `info!("  {line}")` indents every subprocess line under whatever `push <stage>:` line preceded it.

Two notes:

1. **Scope is global, not push-only** — `common::run` is shared by every subcommand (sync, init, finalize, etc.), so all subprocess output gets the 2-space indent. That's almost certainly what you want (tidier output everywhere), but flagging since it's a change beyond just push's surface.

2. **`$EDITOR` is untouched** — push.rs:945 launches the editor with `.status()`, streaming directly to the terminal (vi needs that — can't pipe). So the editor session looks the same as today.

So 0.37.1 is now six changes:

1. Template wording
2. Parser relax
3. Gitignore: fatal in push, auto-write in init + test-fixture
4. Sync: `--check` / `--no-check` rename + fatal-on-action-needed in `--check`
5. Log prefix: `push:STAGE:` → `push STAGE:`
6. Subprocess output: indent stderr 2 spaces in `common::run`

> AGENT

Easy — one line. Subprocess stderr currently goes through `info!` in `common::run`. Change to `debug!` and it's hidden by default, surfaced with `-v`. Bonus: `debug!` already indents per-line in the formatter (logging.rs:94-96), so this change **subsumes #6** — no separate indent edit needed.

But there's a scope question worth deciding before I code:

**`common::run` is shared across every subcommand.** Hiding subprocess stderr globally affects:

- `vc-x1 init` — silences `Initialized repo in "."` + `Hint: git clean -xdf will remove .jj/`. (User probably wants to see init's confirmation today.)
- `vc-x1 sync --no-check` — silences `Rebased N commits` from the rebase subprocess.
- `vc-x1 finalize` — silences `jj squash` + `jj git push` confirmations.
- `vc-x1 clone` — silences `jj git clone` progress.

For push, hiding is exactly what you want — high-level `push <stage>:` lines tell the story; jj's chatter is debug detail. For init/finalize/clone, the subprocess message often *is* the confirmation the user is waiting for.

Three choices:

1. **Global hide.** Simplest. After this lands, init/finalize/clone get quiet by default; their critical messages move to the subcommand's own `info!` calls if/when dogfooding shows they're missed. Trust-but-verify approach.
2. **Push-only.** Add a `quiet: bool` field to a new `RunOpts` (or a sibling `run_quiet` helper). Push opts in; everyone else stays loud. More code, no surprise behavior changes elsewhere.
3. **Default loud, opt-in quiet via env or config.** Heaviest, probably overkill for now.

My instinct: **(1) global hide**. It matches your earlier "less noisy" framing, and `-v` is a well-known escape hatch. If init feels too quiet, we add an explicit `info!("init: created <path>")` in init.rs at that moment — better UX than the raw jj passthrough anyway.

Your call. Once you pick, I start coding all six (well, five — #6 collapses).

> DEVELOPER

I agree with option 1

9	/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.
10	///
11	/// Default is dry-run: reports per-repo state without mutating. Pass
12	/// `--no-dry-run` to act. On any failure the starting state of every
13	/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.
14	///
15	/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
16	/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects
17	/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.
18	#[derive(Args, Debug)]
19	pub struct SyncArgs {
20	    /// Actually perform the sync (default: dry-run)
21	    #[arg(long)]
22	    pub no_dry_run: bool,
23	
24	    /// Suppress all informational output (exit code signals result)
25	    #[arg(short, long)]
26	    pub quiet: bool,
27	
28	    /// Bookmark to sync in each repo
29	    #[arg(long, default_value = "main")]
30	    pub bookmark: String,
31	
32	    /// Remote to sync against
33	    #[arg(long, default_value = "origin")]
34	    pub remote: String,
35	
36	    /// Path to jj repo; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`]
37	    #[arg(short = 'R', long = "repo", value_name = "PATH")]
38	    pub repos: Vec<PathBuf>,
39	}
40	
41	/// Default repo set when `-R` isn't given — the canonical dual-repo pair.
42	const DEFAULT_REPOS: [&str; 2] = [".", ".claude"];
43	
44	/// Resolve the caller's `-R` flags into a concrete repo list.
45	///
46	/// Empty input (no `-R` given) falls back to `DEFAULT_REPOS`. Each
47	/// provided value is also split on `,` and trimmed, so
48	/// `-R .,.claude` works identically to `-R . -R .claude`.
49	fn resolve_repos(raw: &[PathBuf]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
50	    if raw.is_empty() {
51	        return DEFAULT_REPOS.iter().map(PathBuf::from).collect();
52	    }
53	    raw.iter()
54	        .flat_map(|p| {
55	            p.to_string_lossy()
56	                .split(',')
57	                .map(|s| PathBuf::from(s.trim()))
58	                .collect::<Vec<_>>()
59	        })
60	        .collect()
61	}
62	
63	/// Relationship between a local bookmark and its remote counterpart.
64	#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
65	pub enum State {
66	    /// Local and remote point at the same commit.
67	    UpToDate,
68	    /// Local is a strict ancestor of remote — fast-forward possible.
69	    Behind { local: String, remote: String },
70	    /// Remote is a strict ancestor of local — nothing to pull in.
71	    Ahead { local: String, remote: String },
72	    /// Neither is an ancestor of the other — needs rebase.
73	    Diverged { local: String, remote: String },
74	    /// The bookmark has no `@<remote>` counterpart.
75	    NoRemote,
76	}
77	
78	/// Per-repo context accumulated between the snapshot and action phases.
79	#[derive(Debug)]
80	struct RepoCtx {
81	    path: PathBuf,
82	    #[allow(dead_code)]
83	    op_id: String,
84	    state: State,
85	}
86	
87	/// CLI entry point for the `sync` subcommand.
88	///
89	/// Thin wrapper over `sync_repos` that resolves the `-R` flag into a
90	/// concrete repo list (falling back to the dual-repo default) and
91	/// forwards the rest of the args. Tests call `sync_repos` directly
92	/// with absolute fixture paths.
93	///

95	/// to `Warn` for the duration of the call and restored on return, so

284	/// Perform the mutation corresponding to `ctx.state` when `--no-dry-run`.
285	///
286	/// - `UpToDate` / `Ahead` / `NoRemote` → no-op (and no output, the state
287	///   was already logged by `log_state`).
288	/// - `Behind` → `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` to fast-forward.
289	/// - `Diverged` → `jj rebase -b <b> -d <b>@<remote>`, then probe
290	///   `conflicts()`. A non-empty result means the rebase produced
291	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
292	///   pre-fetch state.
293	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
294	    let repo = &ctx.path;
295	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", args.bookmark, args.remote);
296	    match &ctx.state {
297	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
298	        State::Behind { .. } => {
299	            if args.no_dry_run {
300	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), args.bookmark);
301	                run(
302	                    "jj",
303	                    &[
304	                        "bookmark",
305	                        "set",
306	                        &args.bookmark,
307	                        "-r",
308	                        &remote_rev,
309	                        "-R",
310	                        &repo_str(repo),
311	                    ],
312	                    Path::new("."),
313	                )?;
314	            }
315	            Ok(())
316	        }
317	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
318	            if args.no_dry_run {
319	                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list
320	                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head
321	                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The
322	                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence
323	                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head
324	                // and freshly-fetched remote head).
325	                let local_head = local
326	                    .split(',')
327	                    .find(|h| *h != remote)
328	                    .unwrap_or(local.as_str());

764	    /// Add a local-only commit on `main` in `repo` (not pushed), then
765	    /// restore `@` to an empty child so pre-flight still passes.
766	    ///
767	    /// Sequence: write file → describe `@` → advance `main` to `@` →
768	    /// create a fresh empty `@` above it.
769	    fn add_local_commit(repo: &Path, file: &str, content: &str, msg: &str) {
770	        fs::write(repo.join(file), content).expect("write local file");
771	        jj(repo, &["describe", "@", "-m", msg]);
772	        jj(repo, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
773	        jj(repo, &["new"]);
774	    }
775	
776	    /// Clone `remote_url` into `<base>/<work_name>`, add a commit, push it.
777	    ///
778	    /// Used to make the remote advance beyond the fixture's `main`
779	    /// from a separate working copy. Returns the pushed commit's id.
780	    fn push_from_clone(
781	        base: &Path,
782	        remote_url: &Path,
783	        work_name: &str,
784	        file: &str,
785	        content: &str,
786	        msg: &str,
787	    ) -> String {
788	        let workdir = base.join(work_name);
789	        let out = Command::new("jj")
790	            .args(["git", "clone", "--colocate"])
791	            .arg(remote_url)
792	            .arg(&workdir)
793	            .output()
794	            .expect("spawn jj clone");
795	        assert!(
796	            out.status.success(),
797	            "jj git clone failed: {}",
798	            String::from_utf8_lossy(&out.stderr)
799	        );
800	        fs::write(workdir.join(file), content).expect("write remote file");
801	        jj(&workdir, &["describe", "@", "-m", msg]);
802	        jj(&workdir, &["bookmark", "set", "main", "-r", "@"]);
803	        jj(&workdir, &["git", "push", "--bookmark", "main"]);
804	        cid(&workdir, "main")
805	    }

  "old_string": "/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.\n///\n/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:\n/// - **stderr at `info!`** on success — jj prints human-readable messages\n///   (`Moved 1 bookmarks to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) to\n///   stderr, and the user needs to see them without requiring `-v`.\n/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data\n///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), so `info!` would flood the user\n///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.\n/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's\n///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.\npub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let args_str = args.join(\" \");\n    debug!(\"$ {cmd} {args_str}\");\n    trace!(\"cwd: {}\", cwd.display());\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(cwd)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run {cmd}: {e}\"))?;\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();\n    if !stdout.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"  {stdout}\");\n    }\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    if !stderr.is_empty() {\n        info!(\"{stderr}\");\n    }\n    Ok(stdout)\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Run a shell command. Returns stdout on success.\n///\n/// Logs the command at `debug!` and the process streams as follows:\n/// - **stderr at `debug!`** on success — jj's chatter (`Moved 1 bookmarks\n///   to …`, `Rebased N commits`, `Nothing changed.`) is hidden by default\n///   and surfaced with `-v`. The debug formatter indents each line two\n///   spaces so subprocess output sits visually under the caller's own\n///   `info!` line in `-v` mode.\n/// - **stdout at `debug!`** — callers usually consume stdout as data\n///   (bookmark lists, commit IDs, etc.), and `info!` would flood the user\n///   with machine-readable output they didn't ask for.\n/// - **Failures propagate as `Err`** carrying the stderr; the caller's\n///   error handler (`main::run_command`) surfaces it at `error!`.\npub fn run(cmd: &str, args: &[&str], cwd: &Path) -> Result<String, Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let args_str = args.join(\" \");\n    debug!(\"$ {cmd} {args_str}\");\n    trace!(\"cwd: {}\", cwd.display());\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(cmd)\n        .args(args)\n        .current_dir(cwd)\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"failed to run {cmd}: {e}\"))?;\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout).trim().to_string();\n    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string();\n    if !stdout.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"{stdout}\");\n    }\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(format!(\"{cmd} {args_str} failed: {stderr}\").into());\n    }\n    if !stderr.is_empty() {\n        debug!(\"{stderr}\");\n    }\n    Ok(stdout)\n}"
}

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/push.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Log a warning when the configured state path isn't matched by a\n/// `.gitignore` entry. The check is intentionally simple — looks\n/// for `<state_dir>` or `/<state_dir>` as a line in `.gitignore`\n/// at the repo root. Misses trickier patterns (nested wildcards,\n/// ignore files further down the tree) but catches the common\n/// \"user changed the config and forgot to update .gitignore\"\n/// case, which is the whole point.\nfn check_gitignore_coherence(root: &Path, state_dir_name: &str) {\n    let gitignore = root.join(\".gitignore\");\n    let matched = match fs::read_to_string(&gitignore) {\n        Ok(content) => content.lines().any(|line| {\n            let l = line.trim();\n            l == state_dir_name\n                || l == format!(\"/{state_dir_name}\")\n                || l == format!(\"{state_dir_name}/\")\n                || l == format!(\"/{state_dir_name}/\")\n        }),\n        Err(_) => false,\n    };\n    if !matched {\n        warn!(\n            \"push: state dir '{state_dir_name}' is not in {} — \\\n             add a '/{state_dir_name}' line so in-progress push-state \\\n             files don't get committed\",\n            gitignore.display()\n        );\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Verify the configured state dir is matched by a `.gitignore` entry,\n/// erroring if not. The check is intentionally simple — looks for\n/// `<state_dir>` or `/<state_dir>` as a line in `.gitignore` at the\n/// repo root. Misses trickier patterns (nested wildcards, ignore files\n/// further down the tree) but catches the common \"user changed the\n/// config and forgot to update .gitignore\" case, which is the whole\n/// point. Fatal as of 0.37.1 — the warning was easy to miss in the\n/// preflight wall of output, and a committed state file is a real\n/// foot-gun. `vc-x1 init` and `vc-x1 test-fixture` write `/.vc-x1`\n/// into their generated `.gitignore` so the fresh-repo path is clean.\nfn check_gitignore_coherence(\n    root: &Path,\n    state_dir_name: &str,\n) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {\n    let gitignore = root.join(\".gitignore\");\n    let matched = match fs::read_to_string(&gitignore) {\n        Ok(content) => content.lines().any(|line| {\n            let l = line.trim();\n            l == state_dir_name\n                || l == format!(\"/{state_dir_name}\")\n                || l == format!(\"{state_dir_name}/\")\n                || l == format!(\"/{state_dir_name}/\")\n        }),\n        Err(_) => false,\n    };\n    if !matched {\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"push: state dir '{state_dir_name}' is not in {} — \\\n             add a '/{state_dir_name}' line so in-progress push-state \\\n             files don't get committed\",\n            gitignore.display()\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}"
}
```

243	/// Ensure `@` is a descendant of `bookmark`, rebasing if not.
244	///
245	/// `jj git fetch` fast-forwards a tracked local bookmark to the remote
246	/// tip when local is a strict ancestor, but leaves `@` behind — still
247	/// parented on the *pre-fetch* bookmark commit. The `.claude` repo is
248	/// the motivating case: trailing session writes (e.g. `/exit`'s jsonl
249	/// tail) sit in `@`, and without this step they'd end up orphaned on a
250	/// stale branch when the remote advanced.
251	///
252	/// Skipped when `main::@` is already non-empty (i.e., `@` is already
253	/// reachable from `bookmark`). On `--no-dry-run`, runs
254	/// `jj rebase -b @ -d <bookmark>` which carries all commits between
255	/// the old parent and `@` forward, then checks `conflicts()` — any
256	/// conflict in this step trips the outer revert the same way a
257	/// conflicted `act_on_state` rebase would.
258	fn ensure_at_on_main(
259	    repo: &Path,
260	    bookmark: &str,
261	    apply: bool,
262	) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
263	    if revset_nonempty(repo, &format!("{bookmark}::@"))? {
264	        return Ok(());
265	    }
266	    info!("{}: rebasing @ onto '{bookmark}'", repo.display());
267	    if apply {
268	        run(
269	            "jj",
270	            &["rebase", "-b", "@", "-d", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str(repo)],
271	            Path::new("."),
272	        )?;
273	        if has_conflicts(repo)? {
274	            return Err(format!(
275	                "{}: rebase of @ onto '{bookmark}' produced conflicts",
276	                repo.display()
277	            )
278	            .into());
279	        }
280	    }
281	    Ok(())
282	}

On any failure the starting state of every\n/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.\n///\n/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and\n/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects\n/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SyncArgs {\n    /// Actually perform the sync (default: dry-run)\n    #[arg(long)]\n    pub no_dry_run: bool,",
  "new_string": "/// Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.\n///\n/// Default is `--check`: fetch + classify + report, **error** if any\n/// repo is `behind` or `diverged`. Pass `--no-check` to actually\n/// rebase / fast-forward. On any failure the starting state of every\n/// repo is restored via `jj op restore`.\n///\n/// Scripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`\n/// explicitly rather than relying on the default — defaults can shift,\n/// explicit flags lock in the contract.\n///\n/// Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and\n/// `.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo projects\n/// (e.g. `vc-template-x1`) or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces.\n#[derive(Args, Debug)]\npub struct SyncArgs {\n    /// Verify only — fetch + classify; error if any repo needs action.\n    /// This is the default; pass explicitly in scripts.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"no_check\")]\n    pub check: bool,\n\n    /// Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase / fast-forward as needed.\n    #[arg(long, conflicts_with = \"check\")]\n    pub no_check: bool,"
}

  "new_string": "    // Phase 3 — act (subprocess output streams through as usual).\n    // `act_on_state` and `ensure_at_on_main` short-circuit when\n    // `args.no_check` is false, so check mode is a true no-op here.\n    for ctx in &ctxs {\n        act_on_state(ctx, args)?;\n        ensure_at_on_main(&ctx.path, &args.bookmark, args.no_check)?;\n    }\n\n    // Phase 4 — check mode is fatal when action would be needed.\n    // Apply mode (`--no-check`) ran the action above and is done.\n    if !args.no_check && any_action_needed {\n        let n_action = ctxs\n            .iter()\n            .filter(|c| matches!(c.state, State::Behind { .. } | State::Diverged { .. }))\n            .count();\n        let noun = if n_action == 1 { \"repo\" } else { \"repos\" };\n        return Err(format!(\n            \"sync: {n_action} {noun} need action (see above) — \\\n             resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run\"\n        )\n        .into());\n    }\n    Ok(())\n}"
}

305	/// Perform the mutation corresponding to `ctx.state` when `--no-check`.
306	///
307	/// - `UpToDate` / `Ahead` / `NoRemote` → no-op (and no output, the state
308	///   was already logged by `log_state`).
309	/// - `Behind` → `jj bookmark set <b> -r <b>@<remote>` to fast-forward.
310	/// - `Diverged` → `jj rebase -b <b> -d <b>@<remote>`, then probe
311	///   `conflicts()`. A non-empty result means the rebase produced
312	///   conflicted commits; return `Err` so the outer revert restores the
313	///   pre-fetch state.
314	fn act_on_state(ctx: &RepoCtx, args: &SyncArgs) -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
315	    let repo = &ctx.path;
316	    let remote_rev = format!("{}@{}", args.bookmark, args.remote);
317	    match &ctx.state {
318	        State::UpToDate | State::Ahead { .. } | State::NoRemote => Ok(()),
319	        State::Behind { .. } => {
320	            if args.no_dry_run {
321	                info!("{}: fast-forwarding '{}'", repo.display(), args.bookmark);
322	                run(
323	                    "jj",
324	                    &[
325	                        "bookmark",
326	                        "set",
327	                        &args.bookmark,
328	                        "-r",
329	                        &remote_rev,
330	                        "-R",
331	                        &repo_str(repo),
332	                    ],
333	                    Path::new("."),
334	                )?;
335	            }
336	            Ok(())
337	        }
338	        State::Diverged { local, remote } => {
339	            if args.no_dry_run {
340	                // `local` is either a single commit id or a comma-joined list
341	                // of heads when the bookmark is conflicted. Pick the head
342	                // that isn't the remote — that's the local-only tip. The
343	                // comma-joined path covers the jj post-fetch divergence
344	                // shape (local bookmark conflicted between old local head
345	                // and freshly-fetched remote head).
346	                let local_head = local
347	                    .split(',')
```

```
405	On any failure — conflicted rebase, subprocess error, anything — `sync`
406	restores every repo to its starting state via `jj op restore`. Either
407	every repo advances or none do. Working-copy files are preserved
408	across the revert: jj rewinds the operation log but leaves disk
409	content untouched, and any conflicted commits introduced by the failed
410	rebase are abandoned on the way back.
411	
412	```
413	vc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo default, dry-run
414	vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run               # dual-repo, act
415	vc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project
416	vc-x1 sync -R .,.claude -R ../other   # mixed: repeat + comma-separate
417	```
418	
419	| Flag | Description |
420	|------|-------------|
421	| `-R, --repo <PATH>` | Repo to sync; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`] |
422	| `--no-dry-run` | Apply; without it, classify and report only |
423	| `-q, --quiet` | Suppress all output; exit code signals result (for scripts) |
424	| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Bookmark to sync in each repo [default: main] |
425	| `--remote <NAME>` | Remote to sync against [default: origin] |
426	
427	**Output shape.** Sync collapses output based on what it finds:
428	
429	- **All up-to-date** — one-line summary:
430	  `sync: N repos, all up-to-date`. Nothing else. Makes "sprinkle
431	  sync everywhere" genuinely cheap.
432	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) — per-repo fetch +
433	  state lines, then `dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply`
434	  (or the actual actions on `--no-dry-run`).
435	- **`--quiet`** — no output at any level; exit code is the only
436	  signal. Intended for scripts that just need success/failure.
437	
438	**Note on the `behind` case.** jj's `git fetch` already fast-forwards a
439	tracked local bookmark when it's a strict ancestor of the incoming
440	remote, so in the common case `sync` reports `up-to-date` rather than
441	`behind`. The `behind` branch covers untracked bookmarks and edge
442	configs where auto-advance is disabled.
443	
444	### finalize
445	
446	Atomically squash, set bookmark, and push a jj repo. The primary use
447	case is the bot finalizing its own session repo (`.claude`) at the end
448	of a session. The bot can't just `jj commit` because the act of
449	committing generates more session data — files written after the commit
450	would be lost. `finalize` solves this by:
451	
452	1. **`--detach`**: spawning a background process so the bot session can
453	   end immediately (no more writes after the bot exits)
454	2. **`--delay`**: waiting for trailing writes to settle
```

Nothing else. Makes \"sprinkle\n  sync everywhere\" genuinely cheap.\n- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) — per-repo fetch +\n  state lines, then `dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply`\n  (or the actual actions on `--no-dry-run`).\n- **`--quiet`** — no output at any level; exit code is the only\n  signal. Intended for scripts that just need success/failure.",
  "new_string": "```\nvc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo default, --check (verify)\nvc-x1 sync --check                    # explicit form of the default\nvc-x1 sync --no-check                 # dual-repo, apply\nvc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project\nvc-x1 sync -R .,.claude -R ../other   # mixed: repeat + comma-separate\n```\n\nScripts and automation should pass `--check` or `--no-check`\nexplicitly rather than rely on the default — defaults can shift,\nexplicit flags lock in the contract. Interactive use can take the\ndefault.\n\n| Flag | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `-R, --repo <PATH>` | Repo to sync; repeatable or comma-separated [default: `.,.claude`] |\n| `--check` | Verify only — fetch + classify, error if any repo needs action (default) |\n| `--no-check` | Apply — fetch + classify, then rebase/fast-forward as needed |\n| `-q, --quiet` | Suppress all output; exit code signals result (for scripts) |\n| `--bookmark <NAME>` | Bookmark to sync in each repo [default: main] |\n| `--remote <NAME>` | Remote to sync against [default: origin] |\n\n**Output shape.** Sync collapses output based on what it finds:\n\n- **All up-to-date** — one-line summary:\n  `sync: N repos, all up-to-date`. Nothing else. Makes \"sprinkle\n  sync everywhere\" genuinely cheap.\n- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`) — per-repo fetch +\n  state lines, then **fatal in `--check` mode** with\n  `sync: N repos need action — resolve with vc-x1 sync --no-check\n  and re-run`. Under `--no-check`, the actions run instead.\n- **`--quiet`** — no output at any level; exit code is the only\n  signal. Intended for scripts that just need success/failure."

371	
372	### Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)
373	
374	Push's preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run` as its first step
375	so divergence is resolved before the build. Running sync manually
376	before you *start* editing is still cheap (one line when clean)
377	and surfaces remote changes earlier:
378	
379	```
380	vc-x1 sync                            # dual-repo workspace
381	vc-x1 sync -R .                       # single-repo project
382	vc-x1 sync --quiet                    # silent; exit code signals result
383	```
384	
385	Output shape:
386	
387	- **Clean**: one line — `sync: N repos, all up-to-date`. Proceed.
388	- **Action needed** (`behind` / `diverged`): per-repo fetch +
389	  state lines, then `dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply`.
390	  Inspect, then re-run with `--no-dry-run` (or resolve conflicts
391	  if rebase fails).
392	- **`--quiet`**: no output; exit code is the only signal.
393	
394	### After finalize: stop and wait
```

555	
556	## Tighten `$EDITOR` template wording (0.37.1)
557	
558	First dogfood of the interactive `vc-x1 push` flow exposed two
559	papercuts in the editor template:
560	
561	1. The header was structured "title on first line / blank line /
562	   body after" — easy to read as "title now, body in a second
563	   prompt later". A user typed only the title, `:wq`'d, and was
564	   surprised when push proceeded with an empty body instead of
565	   re-prompting.
566	2. The template required a literal blank line between title and
567	   body. If a user typed title-then-body with no blank between,
568	   `parse_message`'s `splitn(2, "\n\n")` made the *whole thing*
569	   the title and the body empty — silent footgun.
570	
571	The fix is to make the template's contract explicit and to relax
572	the parser so the blank-line separator is not user-required.
573	
574	- `src/push.rs` — template header rewritten:
575	
576	  > Leave as-is to abort. Enter the Title on the first line,
577	  > optionally followed by the Body on subsequent lines. The
578	  > blank line between Title and Body is inserted automatically,
579	  > and the ochid trailer is appended per-repo automatically —
580	  > don't add either here.
581	  > Lines starting with `#` are ignored.
582	
583	  States the contract up front (one editor session, title on
584	  line 1, body optional after) and surfaces the two
585	  auto-insertions (separator blank line, ochid trailer) so the
586	  user knows not to type either.
587	
588	- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message` rewritten: first non-comment
589	  line is the title, remainder (trimmed) is the body. Blank
590	  line between is allowed but not required. Doc comment updated.
591	  The "blank line between title and body in the final commit
592	  message" responsibility lives in the commit-stage `format!`
593	  calls (`"{title}\n\n{body}\n\nochid: ..."`), which were
594	  already there — so no change needed downstream of the parser.
595	
596	- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message_cases` test grows two cases:
597	  title+body with no blank line (new behavior), and a body
598	  with internal blank lines preserved (regression guard for
599	  the multi-paragraph body case the old splitn-based code
600	  also handled correctly but is now exercised by a different
601	  code path).
602	
603	- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — version `0.37.0` → `0.37.1`.
604	
605	- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry added.
606	
607	**Why single-step `0.37.1` rather than `0.37.0-6`:** the 0.37.0
608	arc was closed by the done marker; this is a focused mechanical
609	fix discovered during first-real-use, not a continuation of the
610	push design work. Keeps the dev ladder of a feature distinct
611	from post-ship polish.
612	
```

  "old_string": "## Tighten `$EDITOR` template wording (0.37.1)\n\nFirst dogfood of the interactive `vc-x1 push` flow exposed two\npapercuts in the editor template:\n\n1. The header was structured \"title on first line / blank line /\n   body after\" — easy to read as \"title now, body in a second\n   prompt later\". A user typed only the title, `:wq`'d, and was\n   surprised when push proceeded with an empty body instead of\n   re-prompting.\n2. The template required a literal blank line between title and\n   body. If a user typed title-then-body with no blank between,\n   `parse_message`'s `splitn(2, \"\\n\\n\")` made the *whole thing*\n   the title and the body empty — silent footgun.\n\nThe fix is to make the template's contract explicit and to relax\nthe parser so the blank-line separator is not user-required.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — template header rewritten:\n\n  > Leave as-is to abort. Enter the Title on the first line,\n  > optionally followed by the Body on subsequent lines. The\n  > blank line between Title and Body is inserted automatically,\n  > and the ochid trailer is appended per-repo automatically —\n  > don't add either here.\n  > Lines starting with `#` are ignored.\n\n  States the contract up front (one editor session, title on\n  line 1, body optional after) and surfaces the two\n  auto-insertions (separator blank line, ochid trailer) so the\n  user knows not to type either.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message` rewritten: first non-comment\n  line is the title, remainder (trimmed) is the body. Blank\n  line between is allowed but not required. Doc comment updated.\n  The \"blank line between title and body in the final commit\n  message\" responsibility lives in the commit-stage `format!`\n  calls (`\"{title}\\n\\n{body}\\n\\nochid: ...\"`), which were\n  already there — so no change needed downstream of the parser.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message_cases` test grows two cases:\n  title+body with no blank line (new behavior), and a body\n  with internal blank lines preserved (regression guard for\n  the multi-paragraph body case the old splitn-based code\n  also handled correctly but is now exercised by a different\n  code path).\n\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — version `0.37.0` → `0.37.1`.\n\n- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry added.\n\n**Why single-step `0.37.1` rather than `0.37.0-6`:** the 0.37.0\narc was closed by the done marker; this is a focused mechanical\nfix discovered during first-real-use, not a continuation of the\npush design work. Keeps the dev ladder of a feature distinct\nfrom post-ship polish.",
  "new_string": "## First-dogfood polish for push (0.37.1)\n\nFirst dogfood of the interactive `vc-x1 push` flow exposed six\npapercuts. They're all small, all surfaced in the same session, and\nall share the same \"post-ship polish\" framing — so they ship as one\nsingle-step `0.37.1` rather than a `0.37.0-6` continuation. The\n0.37.0 arc was closed by the done marker; this keeps the dev ladder\nof the feature distinct from sandpaper.\n\n### 1. `$EDITOR` template wording + parser relaxation\n\nThe header was structured \"title on first line / blank line / body\nafter\" — easy to read as \"title now, body in a second prompt later\".\nA user typed only the title, `:wq`'d, and was surprised when push\nproceeded with an empty body instead of re-prompting. The template\nalso required a literal blank line between title and body — if a\nuser typed title-then-body with no blank between, `parse_message`'s\n`splitn(2, \"\\n\\n\")` made the *whole thing* the title. Silent\nfootgun.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — template header rewritten:\n\n  > Leave as-is to abort. Enter the Title on the first line,\n  > optionally followed by the Body on subsequent lines. The\n  > blank line between Title and Body is inserted automatically,\n  > and the ochid trailer is appended per-repo automatically —\n  > don't add either here.\n  > Lines starting with `#` are ignored.\n\n  States the contract up front (one editor session, title on line\n  1, body optional after) and surfaces the two auto-insertions\n  (separator blank line, ochid trailer) so the user knows not to\n  type either.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message` rewritten: first non-comment\n  line = title, remainder (trimmed) = body. Blank line between is\n  allowed but not required. The \"blank line between title and body\n  in the final commit message\" responsibility lives in the\n  commit-stage `format!` calls (`\"{title}\\n\\n{body}\\n\\nochid: ...\"`),\n  which were already there.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `parse_message_cases` grows two new cases:\n  title+body with no blank line (new behavior), and a multi-line\n  body with internal blank lines (regression guard).\n\n### 2. Gitignore coherence: fatal in push, auto-write in init + fixture\n\nThe gitignore-coherence check was a `warn!` — easy to miss in the\npreflight wall of output. A committed push-state file is a real\nfoot-gun (jj would track session-only state into history). Promote\nto fatal, and have `init` and `test-fixture` ship `/.vc-x1` in their\ngenerated `.gitignore` so the fresh-repo path is clean.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `check_gitignore_coherence` returns\n  `Result<(), Box<dyn Error>>` and `push_in` propagates with `?`.\n  Error message names the file and the line to add. No bypass\n  flag; the fix is one line.\n\n- `src/init.rs` — `GITIGNORE_CODE` adds `/.vc-x1`; matching test\n  asserts the new line.\n\n- `src/test_fixture.rs` — `GITIGNORE_CODE` adds `/.vc-x1`; matching\n  test asserts the new line. Push integration tests use this\n  fixture, so the now-fatal check passes there transparently.\n\n- `README.md` — fixture-layout snippet adds `/.vc-x1`.\n\n### 3. `vc-x1 sync`: `--check` / `--no-check` rename + fatal check mode\n\nPush's preflight ran `vc-x1 sync --no-dry-run`, which auto-rebased\nthe local repo against the remote *before* the user reached the\nreview/message gates. A surprise rebase mid-push is exactly the\nkind of unsupervised mutation the gates are meant to prevent.\n\nRename `--no-dry-run` to `--no-check` and `--dry-run` to `--check`\n(the implicit default; passable explicitly). Check mode is now\n**fatal** when any repo is `behind`/`diverged` — the user must\nresolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run. Push's preflight\ncalls `vc-x1 sync --check` so divergence surfaces early but never\nrewrites local state without explicit consent.\n\nThe bot uses explicit forms in scripts and automation (per\n\"defaults can shift, explicit flags lock in the contract\"); the\ninteractive user can rely on the default.\n\n- `src/sync.rs` — `SyncArgs.no_dry_run: bool` → two fields\n  `check: bool` and `no_check: bool`, mutually `conflicts_with`.\n  Field `args.no_dry_run` → `args.no_check` everywhere internally\n  (semantic preserved: true = act). New phase-4 logic: in check\n  mode (`!args.no_check`) with `any_action_needed`, return Err\n  with explicit \"resolve with `vc-x1 sync --no-check` and re-run\"\n  message and a per-repo state recap above (already printed by\n  phase 2).\n\n- `src/sync.rs` — `parse_defaults` / `parse_overrides` tests\n  updated; new `parse_check_flag` and `parse_check_no_check_conflict`\n  tests exercise the new flag surface.\n\n- `src/sync.rs` — `apply_args()` test helper updated to\n  `{ check: false, no_check: true, ... }`.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — `stage_preflight` calls `vc-x1 sync --check`\n  (was `--no-dry-run`). Doc comment rewritten to explain why\n  check mode (no auto-rebase before the gates).\n\n- `CLAUDE.md` — \"Pre-step: `vc-x1 sync` (still useful)\" section\n  rewritten around `--check`/`--no-check`. Output-shape bullets\n  updated to describe the fatal check-mode error.\n\n- `README.md` — sync usage examples + flag table + output-shape\n  section updated. Push-stage table's `preflight` row updated to\n  `vc-x1 sync --check`.\n\n### 4. Stage-prefix log format: `push:STAGE:` → `push STAGE:`\n\nStage-tagged log lines were `push:preflight:`, `push:review:`,\n… `push:finalize-claude:`. With nine prefixes plus subprocess\noutput, the colon-pair felt dense; a single space between \"push\"\nand the stage parses faster.\n\n- `src/push.rs` — nine stage prefixes renamed (preflight,\n  review, message, commit-app, commit-claude, bookmark-both,\n  push-app, finalize-claude, step). Bare `push: ...` lines\n  (top-level, no stage) are unchanged.\n\n  Side note: `push push-app:` now reads with the word \"push\"\n  twice in a row. The stage's name is `push-app` because its\n  job is to push the app repo; renaming it would touch the\n  state-file format and `--from` values, which is wider surgery\n  than warranted here.\n\n### 5. Subprocess output hidden by default (visible with `-v`)\n\nSubprocess stderr (jj's `Working copy now at ...`, `Rebased N\ncommits`, etc.) was emitted at `info!` in `common::run`, so it\nshowed by default. Combined with the dense `push:STAGE:` prefix\nand the multi-stage flow, the output felt noisy. Drop subprocess\nstderr to `debug!` — hidden by default, surfaced with `-v`. The\ndebug formatter already indents per-line two spaces, so when `-v`\nis set, subprocess lines sit visually under the caller's `info!`\nheader. This change subsumes the originally-planned \"indent\nstderr two spaces in info!\" fix.\n\n- `src/common.rs` — `run` doc comment rewritten. Subprocess\n  stderr success path: `info!(\"{stderr}\")` → `debug!(\"{stderr}\")`.\n  Subprocess stdout: `debug!(\"  {stdout}\")` → `debug!(\"{stdout}\")`\n  (drop the redundant inline indent — the formatter already\n  indents debug lines).\n\n  Scope note: `common::run` is shared by every subcommand, so this\n  silences subprocess output globally (init, sync, finalize, clone,\n  push). Trade-off accepted: high-volume jj chatter is rarely\n  load-bearing for the user, and `-v` brings it back. If a specific\n  subcommand needs a particular subprocess line surfaced, the right\n  fix is an explicit `info!` in that subcommand at the moment the\n  thing happens — not relying on subprocess passthrough.\n\n### 6. Versioning + bookkeeping\n\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — version `0.37.0` → `0.37.1`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry added (covers all 5 fixes)."
}
```

vc-x1 0.37.1

Fetch and sync a set of repos to their remotes.

Repo set defaults to the dual-repo workspace pair (`.` and
`.claude`); override with `-R` / `--repo` for single-repo
projects or arbitrary multi-repo workspaces. Flag is repeatable
or comma-separated (e.g. `-R . -R .claude` or `-R .,.claude`).

Default is dry-run — re-run with --no-dry-run to apply. Per repo:
  - up-to-date        nothing to do
  - behind            fast-forward bookmark to remote
  - ahead             nothing to sync (local has unpushed work)
  - diverged          rebase local onto remote; fail on conflicts
  - no remote         bookmark has no @<remote> counterpart; skip

After the bookmark action, `@` is rebased onto the (possibly
advanced) bookmark if it isn't already a descendant, so trailing
working-copy writes (e.g. `.claude`'s `/exit` tail) don't end up
orphaned on a stale branch.

On any failure, every repo is reverted to its starting state via
`jj op restore`. Working-copy files are preserved across the
revert — the operation log rewinds but disk content stays.
```

> AGENT

Mix-up of subcommand surfaces, not an install issue. `--check` / `--no-check` are on **`vc-x1 sync`**, not `vc-x1 push`:

```
vc-x1 sync -h
```

Push has its own `--dry-run` (preview the push stages without side effects) — different flag, different purpose. Push invokes sync internally with `--check` during preflight; you don't pass it through push.

So:
- `vc-x1 sync --help` → see `--check` / `--no-check`.
- `vc-x1 push --help` → push's own flags (including push's `--dry-run`, which is unchanged).
- `vc-x1 push main` → preflight runs `vc-x1 sync --check` for you; if anything's behind/diverged you get the fatal sync error before any approval gates appear.

Install is current (`vc-x1 0.37.1`). Want to try `vc-x1 sync -h` to confirm the new flags are visible?
