wink@3900x 26-04-22T22:55:24.383Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/tf-1/work ((main))

> AGENT

Good catch. Sync's "up-to-date" is bookmark-vs-remote scope — it intentionally ignores working-copy changes since for the push flow, having `@` non-empty is the whole point (that's what's about to be committed). But the wording reads as broader than it is.

Three ways to fix, increasing in scope:

**A) Wording-only clarification** — change `sync: 2 repos, all up-to-date` to `sync: 2 repos, all bookmarks up-to-date` (or `tracking up-to-date`). Honest about scope, no new logic. Fits 0.37.1.

**B) Add a working-copy summary line** — e.g. `sync: 2 repos, all up-to-date (./: 1 changed file, ./.claude: clean)`. Useful info but requires deciding the format and adding a `jj st`-equivalent probe per repo. Probably 0.37.2.

**C) Treat working-copy changes as "action needed" in `--check`** — fatal if `@` is non-empty. Forces discipline but conflates two concepts (sync vs commit) and would break the push preflight (which deliberately runs sync before its own commit stages). Probably wrong.

My recommendation:

- **(A) for 0.37.1** — quick, cohesive with "scope clarity" theme of the polish pass.
- **(B) as 0.37.2** — get a fresh design conversation about format (per-repo stat? overall count? compactness?), since it touches user-facing output shape.

Sound right? If yes, I'll make the (A) edit and roll it into the same 0.37.1 commit. (B) gets a follow-up todo entry.

> AGENT

Push went through commit-app → commit-claude → bookmark-both → push-app successfully (the **app remote has the commit**). It failed at `finalize-claude` because `.claude`'s `main` bookmark isn't tracking its remote:

```
error: bookmark 'main' has non-tracking remote 'main@origin' —
run `jj bookmark track main --remote=origin -R .../.claude` to fix
```

This is exactly the case the existing top-of-todo entry calls out ("Check for non-tracking-remote bookmarks during push and sync preflight…") — dogfood just hit it.

Current state:
- App repo: commit landed, bookmark advanced, **pushed to remote** ✓
- `.claude` repo: commit landed, bookmark advanced locally, **not pushed**
- Saved push state likely still present (didn't reach completion)

Suggested fix per the error message:

```
jj bookmark track main --remote=origin -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude
vc-x1 finalize --repo /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude --squash --push main --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

(That's the "Manual finalize fallback" recipe from CLAUDE.md, with the bookmark-track step prepended.)

> AGENT

Good catch — there's a separate, bigger problem you spotted. Two distinct issues:

**1. Your real catch: `tf-1/` got committed and pushed to the app remote.**

The app repo's `.gitignore` (above) doesn't list `/tf-1`. jj snapshotted the working copy and folded all 56 fixture files (bare-git remote dirs, hooks, objects, refs) into the commit. The commit landed on the app remote that way.

**2. `.claude` bookmark not tracking — pre-existing, unrelated to tf-1.**

`jj log -R .claude` shows `main` and `main@origin` as separate refs (not a tracked pair). The session repo has been like this since setup; just first time it bit us because it's the first push attempt on `.claude`. Independent from #1.

**Recovery is two halves:**

- **`.claude` side** — track the bookmark, then re-run finalize to push. Conservative.
- **App side** — the tf-1 commit is on the remote. Options:
  - (a) Force-push a fixup that strips `tf-1/*` (destructive, needs your OK).
  - (b) Add `/tf-1` to `.gitignore`, commit a follow-up that removes the tracked tf-1 files (non-destructive, leaves history with the noise commit but cleans current state).
  - (c) Leave tf-1 in history; just add `/tf-1` to `.gitignore` going forward (cheapest, history stays cluttered).

Probably want **(b)** — clean current state without rewriting published history. Force-push (a) is safer in this small project but breaks the "no destructive without explicit ask" default.

Want me to inspect first (`vc-x1 push --status`, etc.) before doing anything? Or pick a recovery path?

Actually hold on — your fixture path was explicit `./tf-1`, while `vc-x1 test-fixture` defaults to `$TMPDIR/vc-x1-test-<ts>`. The explicit in-repo path is the unusual case. Two questions before I touch `.gitignore`:

1. **Should `.gitignore` get `/tf-1` (specific) or `/tf-*` (glob)?** I lean `/tf-*` since you'd probably use `./tf-2`, `./tf-3` next time by habit, but specific is fine if you'd rather keep it minimal.

2. **Should `vc-x1 test-fixture` refuse to scaffold inside the repo?** Separate todo-worthy question — the ergonomic fix is at the tool level, not just `.gitignore`. If `--path ./tf-1` resolved inside the workspace and test-fixture warned/errored, none of us would be in this mess today.

Once `.gitignore` is sorted, the rest is the documented recovery:

```bash
# 3. squash the removal into the just-pushed feat commit
jj squash --ignore-immutable -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1

# 4. bump main to the squashed commit
jj bookmark set main -r @- -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1

# 5. force-push (non-fast-forward; jj will warn/require confirmation)
jj git push --bookmark main -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1
```

> AGENT

Diagnosis from op log + code inspection:

**Finding:** this `.claude` was bootstrapped via `jj git init --colocate` + `jj git fetch` + manual `jj bookmark set main -r main@origin` (see op log entries from 3 hours ago and 23 hours ago). That sequence positions the bookmark but **doesn't establish tracking** with the remote — tracking in jj is a separate explicit operation.

Compare:
- `src/init.rs:482-485` DOES call `jj bookmark track main --remote=origin` after Step 10's re-init. So `vc-x1 init`-created repos get tracking.
- `src/test_fixture.rs` doesn't explicitly track — but test fixtures do the **initial push from scratch** which auto-establishes tracking as a side effect.
- Your `.claude` didn't go through either path cleanly — either predates the current init flow, or was hand-bootstrapped at some point.

So the root-cause story is: **this particular `.claude` missed the tracking step at setup.** One-time fix.

Plus a small bonus observation: your `.claude` has an `autocomplete@origin: (divergent)` from back in 0.26.0 days — unrelated to this issue but worth a future tidy-up.

Ready to run the recovery. Green light for:

```bash
jj bookmark track main --remote=origin -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude
vc-x1 finalize --repo /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude --squash --push main --delay 10 --detach --log /tmp/vc-x1-finalize.log
```

> AGENT

Clarifying finding: `.claude` on **fwlaptop** pushes fine — the op log shows multiple successful `jj git push --bookmark main -R .../.claude` operations (3 hours ago, 23 hours ago, 1 day ago). So tracking IS established on fwlaptop.

Refined diagnosis: **tracking is per-workspace, stored in the local jj store, not in shared git refs.** When you sync between machines, git refs transfer, but jj's per-workspace tracking metadata doesn't. So:

- fwlaptop's `.claude` workspace: tracking established at some earlier setup → pushes work.
- 3900x's `.claude` workspace: tracking never established here → pushes refuse.
- Every `vc-x1 sync` between them fetches refs and updates `main@origin`, but doesn't backfill the tracking flag.

That reshapes the investigation:

wink@3900x 26-04-23T01:14:32.495Z:~/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude ((main))

2026-04-22-14:18:06

> AGENT

Reasonable explanation: gitk by default shows **author date**, not committer date, and jj preserves author dates across squashes.

- File Birth `14:17:44` → jsonl first created when the session started.
- gitk `14:18:06` → author date, stamped when the commit was first *described* (~22s after the jsonl started being written). Stable across every subsequent squash.
- File Modify `18:11:47` → most recent write to the jsonl (ongoing session traffic).
- `jj log` would show **committer** date (~16:08 earlier today for the chores commit; closer to "now" after the finalize squash).

So there's no lost history — the two timestamps just encode different things:

- **Author date**: "when the work was first articulated." jj keeps this pinned to the original commit description, so it ages with the feature's conceptual origin, not with the latest rewrite.
- **Committer date**: "when this specific commit object came into being." Moves forward on every rebase/squash.

`gitk --date=relative` or `gitk --date=iso` + changing the "show author/committer" setting would swap which one displays. Most GUI tools default to author date because it's the more "stable" timestamp in a rebase-heavy workflow. Nothing's wrong here, just jj + git metadata doing their thing.

> DEVELOPER

Where is "Author" date I don't see that in stat's dates?

> AGENT

My bad, sloppy wording. Author date isn't in `stat` at all — it's stored **inside the git commit object**, not on the filesystem.

A commit object looks like:

```
tree <sha>
parent <sha>
author Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1745370486 -0700
committer Wink Saville <wink@saville.com> 1745380694 -0700

<commit message>
```

Those two unix timestamps are recorded by git when the commit is created and live inside the object in `.git/objects/`. gitk reads them from there.

`stat`'s four dates are purely filesystem:

- **Access**: last read
- **Modify**: last write to the file's *bytes*
- **Change**: last write to the *inode* (permissions, size, content)
- **Birth**: inode creation time

Zero overlap with git's author/committer dates — they're two independent recording systems that happen to be near each other in your case because the sequence was: `touch jsonl (Birth 14:17:44)` → `describe commit (author date ~14:18:06)` → …much later… `file still being written (Modify 18:11:47)`.

To see the author date directly:

```bash
git log -1 --format='%ai  author%n%ci  committer' main -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude
# or with jj:
jj log -r main -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude -T 'author.timestamp().ago() ++ " (author)\n" ++ committer.timestamp().ago() ++ " (committer)"'
```

> DEVELOPER

That explained, does it explain the non-tracking bookmark?

> AGENT

No — completely independent systems. Different storage, different replication behavior.

| | Storage | Travels with... |
|---|---|---|
| Author/committer date | Inside the git commit object (`.git/objects/`) | Every clone/fetch/push — content-addressed, immutable |
| Bookmark tracking flag | jj's per-workspace store (`.jj/repo/store/...`) | Nothing. Local to one workspace. |

The author/committer-date discovery was about **commit metadata** (stable, synced everywhere). The non-tracking issue is about **workspace metadata** (mutable, never synced). They don't touch.

Analogy: your chat app syncs messages (the commit object) but not your local "read/unread" flags (the tracking state). Moving to a new device gives you all the messages but resets the read/unread.

So:

- Commits on 3900x and fwlaptop: identical, byte-for-byte. ✓ (author/committer dates included)
- Bookmark tracking on 3900x: not set up here.
- Bookmark tracking on fwlaptop: was set up there at some point.
- `vc-x1 sync` transferring data between them: moves refs and objects, doesn't touch the tracking flag.

The earlier diagnosis stands — fix is still the per-workspace one-time `jj bookmark track` (which we just did on 3900x).

> DEVELOPER

So is sync detecting non-tracking during preflight now?

> AGENT

No — still open. The todo entry I updated earlier captures it:

> Check for non-tracking-remote bookmarks during push and sync preflight…

Current sync's `classify()` only compares local-bookmark position to remote-ref position (→ `Behind` / `Ahead` / `Diverged` / `UpToDate`). It doesn't inspect the tracking flag. A non-tracking bookmark where local==remote would read as `UpToDate`; local-ahead-of-remote (this session's case) reads as `Ahead` (benign, not fatal under `--check`). Either way, the non-tracking condition flies under the radar until `jj git push` actually tries and refuses.

Implementing the detection is small (jj exposes tracking status via `jj bookmark list --tracked` or the template language `remote_bookmark.tracked()`), but the **policy** decision is the interesting part — refuse with a pointer to `jj bookmark track …`, or self-heal by calling track automatically when the remote ref already exists? The todo entry sketches both options; picking one is the next step when you work that item.

> DEVELOPER

I'm also thinking when every command exits we output if main is tracking or not on the "current repo", thoughts?

> AGENT

Honest pushback worth hearing before you commit to it:

**Concern 1 — fights the 0.37.1 quieter-output work.** We just moved subprocess stderr to `debug!` specifically to reduce terminal noise. A per-command always-on tracking line partially undoes that. Commands like `vc-x1 list` or `vc-x1 chid` that are pure reads would grow a trailing bookkeeping line on every invocation.

**Concern 2 — redundant with detect-and-error.** The other todo (the one we just expanded) catches the broken case at the moment it matters: sync/push/finalize refuse with a loud error + exact remediation. If that lands, a passive "tracking: yes" line on every command is low signal — you're watching for "no," and "no" already errors loudly.

**Concern 3 — "current repo" is underdefined in a dual-repo workspace.** What does `vc-x1 chid` print when cwd = project root? Just the app? Both? What if someone runs from `~/` with no repo?

**Three refinements, best-to-worst in my read:**

1. **Silent when correct, loud when broken, on exit of repo-mutating commands.** Catches drift without taxing clean runs. Combined with the preflight detect-and-error, it's belt-and-suspenders on the same signal.

2. **Part of the `vc-x1 status` subcommand** (already in todo as `status|st`). Users who want the snapshot run it; users who don't aren't taxed. Natural home alongside working-copy state + diverged bookmarks.

3. **The always-on version you proposed.** Works, but costs one line on every invocation forever.

My lean: (2) is probably where this belongs long-term — a single place users go when they want "how is my workspace?" — with (1) as a cheap lightweight catch for mutation commands.

Does that reframe it, or do you want the always-on version anyway?

> DEVELOPER

I was suggesting that just while we're looking for the culprit and could add it on entry as well as exit then we absolutely know "which command" is causing it if entry and exit are different, if it something not related to vc-x1 will notify us of that with the "entry" (again temporary).

> AGENT

Ah, that reframes it completely — that's a diagnostic probe, not a permanent feature. Makes total sense, and it's a clean technique:

| Entry | Exit | Culprit |
|---|---|---|
| tracked | tracked | Not this command. |
| tracked | NOT tracked | **This command broke it.** |
| NOT tracked | (any) | Something between the previous command and this one. |
| NOT tracked | tracked | Rare, but interesting — something's repairing it. |

So the probe localizes blame to either "this vc-x1 command" or "something external happened between invocations." Temporary scaffolding, greppable, rip it out once the culprit is caught.

Concrete shape:

```
track-probe enter vc-x1 sync: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked
track-probe exit  vc-x1 sync: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=NOT_TRACKED  ← aha
```

Where:
- **Where**: `main.rs` command dispatcher, before + after `run_command(...)`.
- **What**: tracking status of `main` in both repos of the dual-repo workspace (app at `.`, session at `.claude`). Probes both unconditionally since that's our focus.
- **How**: `jj bookmark list --tracked main -T '<template>' -R <repo>` — machine-readable, no human-output parsing.
- **Output level**: `info!` with a `track-probe:` prefix, so it's visible by default (the whole point is catching the culprit) and easy to `grep -v track-probe` later, AND easy to find/remove when retiring.
- **Graceful degradation**: if the repo doesn't exist, or jj errors, or the bookmark doesn't exist, print `track-probe enter X: unavailable (<reason>)` and continue — don't block the command under investigation.

Scope is small — maybe 30 lines in `main.rs` + a helper. No flag, no config, temporary. Separate commit labeled something like `chore: temp tracking probe on entry/exit (investigation)` with a clear note in the commit body that it'll be reverted once diagnosed.

Green light to implement and commit as a standalone follow-up to 0.37.1?

244	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
245	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
246	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
247	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
248	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
249	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,
250	        Commands::Show(a) => a.common.no_label,
251	        _ => false,
252	    };
253	
254	    if !is_detached_exec {
255	        if !suppress_banner {
256	            // Banner on every normal run, mirroring what `--help`
257	            // shows at the top.
258	            log::info!("{BANNER}");
259	        }
260	        finalize::surface_previous_failures();
261	    }
262	
263	    match cli.command {
264	        Commands::Chid(chid_args) => run_command(chid::chid(&chid_args)),
265	        Commands::Desc(desc_args) => run_command(desc::desc(&desc_args)),
266	        Commands::List(list_args) => run_command(list::list(&list_args)),
267	        Commands::Show(show_args) => run_command(show::show(&show_args)),
268	        Commands::ValidateDesc(validate_desc_args) => {
269	            run_command(validate_desc::validate_desc(&validate_desc_args))
270	        }
271	        Commands::FixDesc(fix_desc_args) => run_command(fix_desc::fix_desc(&fix_desc_args)),
272	        Commands::Clone(clone_args) => run_command(clone::clone_repo(&clone_args)),
273	        Commands::Init(init_args) => run_command(init::init(&init_args)),
274	        Commands::Symlink(symlink_args) => run_command(symlink::symlink(&symlink_args)),
275	        Commands::Sync(sync_args) => run_command(sync::sync(&sync_args)),
276	        Commands::Finalize(finalize_args) => {
277	            let opts = match finalize_args.into_opts(cli.log) {
278	                Ok(opts) => opts,
279	                Err(e) => {
280	                    error!("{e}");
281	                    return ExitCode::FAILURE;
282	                }
283	            };
284	            run_command(finalize::finalize(&opts))
285	        }
286	        Commands::TestFixture(args) => run_command(test_fixture::test_fixture(&args)),
287	        Commands::TestFixtureRm(args) => run_command(test_fixture::test_fixture_rm(&args)),
288	        Commands::Push(push_args) => run_command(push::push(&push_args)),
289	    }
290	}
291

295

297	    fn unknown_command() {
298	        let err = Cli::try_parse_from(["vc-x1", "bogus"])
299	            .unwrap_err()

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/src/main.rs",
  "old_string": "fn run_command(result: Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>) -> ExitCode {\n    match result {\n        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"{e}\");\n            ExitCode::FAILURE\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "fn run_command(result: Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>>) -> ExitCode {\n    match result {\n        Ok(()) => ExitCode::SUCCESS,\n        Err(e) => {\n            error!(\"{e}\");\n            ExitCode::FAILURE\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// TEMPORARY — diagnostic probe for the non-tracking-bookmark issue\n/// surfaced during the 0.37.1 dogfood. Prints `main` bookmark tracking\n/// status in both repos of the dual-repo workspace on entry and exit\n/// of every command. If entry and exit differ, the executing command\n/// is the culprit; if entry is `NOT_TRACKED` but a previous command's\n/// exit was `tracked`, something *between* invocations broke it.\n/// Rip out once the culprit is identified — search for \"track-probe\".\nfn track_probe(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {\n    let repos: [(&Path, &str); 2] = [\n        (Path::new(\".\"), \"app\"),\n        (Path::new(\".claude\"), \".claude\"),\n    ];\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for (repo, label) in repos {\n        if !repo.join(\".jj\").exists() {\n            parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=no-jj\"));\n            continue;\n        }\n        match track_probe_one(repo, \"main\", \"origin\") {\n            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=tracked\")),\n            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED\")),\n            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=err({})\", e.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\"))),\n        }\n    }\n    log::info!(\n        \"track-probe {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}: {}\",\n        parts.join(\", \")\n    );\n}\n\n/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.\n/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` names\n/// `@<remote>`, `Ok(false)` if the bookmark isn't tracking that\n/// remote (or doesn't exist), `Err` on subprocess failure.\nfn track_probe_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {\n    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\n            \"bookmark\",\n            \"list\",\n            \"--tracked\",\n            bookmark,\n            \"-R\",\n            &repo_str,\n        ])\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"spawn: {e}\"))?;\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());\n    }\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);\n    let marker = format!(\"@{remote}:\");\n    Ok(stdout\n        .lines()\n        .any(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(&marker)))\n}"
}

  "new_string": "Probable shape: per-repo \"N pending file(s)\" line under the summary\nwhen non-zero, no flag needed, suppressed by `--quiet`. But the\npush-preflight noise question needs a deliberate answer first.\n\n## Temporary bookmark-tracking diagnostic probe (0.37.2)\n\n0.37.1's first-dogfood push hit a `bookmark 'main' has non-tracking\nremote 'main@origin'` error in the `.claude` repo during the\n`finalize-claude` stage. Recovery was one-time: `jj bookmark track\nmain --remote=origin -R .claude`. But the open question is *when*\nand *why* the tracking flag got lost — the fwlaptop op log shows\n`.claude` pushing fine from there, so the 3900x workspace ended\nup in a different state at some historical point.\n\nThe detect-and-error work (`notes/todo.md` top entry) is the\nlong-term policy fix. This chore is a **temporary diagnostic**\nto localize the culprit: probe `main`-bookmark tracking status on\nentry and exit of every vc-x1 command. If entry and exit differ\nduring a single command, that command is the culprit; if entry\ndiffers from the previous command's exit, something *between*\ninvocations broke it (external tooling, manual jj commands, etc.).\n\nOutput shape:\n\n```\ntrack-probe enter vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked\ntrack-probe exit  vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=NOT_TRACKED\n```\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `use std::path::Path;` added. Two new functions:\n  `track_probe(phase, command_name)` prints the status line for\n  both repos; `track_probe_one(repo, bookmark, remote)` queries\n  jj via `jj bookmark list --tracked <bookmark> -R <repo>`. The\n  tracked-list entry includes a line starting with `@<remote>:`\n  when the bookmark is tracking that remote; absence means\n  not-tracking.\n- `src/main.rs` — dispatch block wraps the `match cli.command`\n  in a `let exit_code = match ...;` binding, with `track_probe`\n  calls immediately before and after. Skipped when `is_detached_exec`\n  (the `finalize --exec` re-entry) since probe noise in that log\n  isn't useful.\n- Graceful degradation — if the repo doesn't exist, missing-`.jj/`\n  detection emits `no-jj`; subprocess failure emits `err(<first line>)`.\n  Never blocks the wrapped command.\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.1` → `0.37.2`.\n\n**How to rip it out** once the culprit is identified:\n\n```bash\ngrep -rn 'track-probe\\|track_probe' src/\n```\n\nThree places to touch:\n1. `src/main.rs` — delete the two functions (`track_probe`,\n   `track_probe_one`) and the `use std::path::Path` import if\n   nothing else needs it.\n2. `src/main.rs` — revert the dispatch to plain\n   `match cli.command { ... }` (drop the `let exit_code = ...`\n   binding, the two `track_probe(...)` calls, and the\n   `command_name` binding).\n3. `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — bump to the next appropriate\n   version.\n\nNet removal: ~80 lines."
}

All green. Summary of the 0.37.2 commit:

- `src/main.rs` — two new helpers (`track_probe`, `track_probe_one`) + entry/exit calls around the dispatch match. Uses `jj bookmark list --tracked main -R <repo>` and checks for `@origin:` line. Graceful degradation: `no-jj` when repo isn't there, `err(<reason>)` on subprocess failure. Skipped under `is_detached_exec`.
- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.1` → `0.37.2`.
- `notes/chores-05.md` — new subsection with rationale, output shape, and a ripout checklist (three touch points, ~80 lines to revert).
- `notes/todo.md` — Done entry for 0.37.2 with `[55]` ref.

Smoke test in the app repo showed `tracked/tracked` both sides, as expected.

Work complete. Please review. On approval I'll prep the commit title and body for a second approval before pushing.

You're right, this is ugly. Two things happened:

1. **The "Invalid tool parameters" you saw** — my first `Bash` tool call had a bogus `command_description` argument and was rejected. I retried; the second call went through. That part wasn't the problem.

2. **The real problem: stale push state.** Look at the output of the successful call — it ran *only* `finalize-claude`, then said "completed all stages (state cleared)":

   ```
   push finalize-claude: vc-x1 finalize --repo ...
   push: completed all stages (state cleared)
   ```

   The state file from the **previous push** (0.37.1's, which errored at finalize-claude before we recovered manually) was still sitting there saying "resume at finalize-claude". So this run resumed there, called finalize (no-op since we'd already finalized manually), and declared itself complete. **It never ran preflight / review / message / commit-app / commit-claude / bookmark-both / push-app.** Your 0.37.2 working copy changes are still uncommitted.

`jj st` confirms — all the edits still dirty:

```
M Cargo.lock
M Cargo.toml
M notes/chores-05.md
M notes/todo.md
M src/main.rs
```

**Root cause:** push's state file isn't cleared when the user recovers out-of-band (manual `vc-x1 finalize`). Push assumes the previous run's halt point is still its halt point, and resumes blindly.

**Fix for this session:** `--restart` to clear the stale state and run from scratch:

```bash
vc-x1 push main --restart --yes --title "$TITLE" --body "$BODY"
```

(I still have `$TITLE` and `$BODY` staged conceptually; would re-assemble the same strings.)

**Systemic followup (new todo):** push should detect stale state on resume. If the saved state's `claude_chid` / `app_chid` no longer match current `@-` or the bookmarks are already past those commits, warn or auto-clear and restart. Worth adding to `notes/todo.md`.

> DEVELOPER

Recovery is easy, but it seems to me our preflight is brittle and maybe error handling in general?

> AGENT

Agreed, and the failures in this session trace back to one design gap. Concrete observations from what we've hit:

**Symptom 1 (0.37.1 push):** failed at `finalize-claude` with a real error (non-tracking bookmark). Push exited; state file parked at `finalize-claude`. But the outside world kept moving — we ran `vc-x1 finalize` manually, then `jj squash --ignore-immutable` + force-push to remove tf-1. Push knew nothing about any of that.

**Symptom 2 (0.37.2 push):** push resumed from the parked state, ran `finalize-claude` (which was a no-op because the world was already finalized), then cheerfully declared "completed all stages (state cleared)" — a lie in spirit. Every earlier stage (preflight, review, commit-app, …) was skipped despite a working copy full of pending changes that needed committing.

**Root cause:** push's state file is assumed authoritative. The state says "resume at X" and push does, without asking whether the repo actually *looks like* it's at stage X. State and reality can diverge through:
- Out-of-band recovery (manual `vc-x1 finalize` / `jj squash`)
- Force-push / history rewrite between runs
- Time passing + the user starting new work
- A previous run's "successful" finish that the state file missed

**Design direction for the systemic fix:**

1. **State-sanity preflight.** On resume, before any stage runs, verify the saved state's expected fingerprint matches reality: does `state.app_chid` still exist? Is `main` at `state.app_chid`'s commit? Are `state.claude_chid` and `state.bookmark` consistent with the repo's current state? If not, refuse with a loud "state is stale — run `vc-x1 push --restart`" error.

2. **Stage prerequisites.** Each stage declares what it expects (working-copy dirty for commit-app? bookmark at specific commit for bookmark-both? etc.). The dispatcher checks before running. Makes "this stage is actually a no-op because the work is done" detectable, not silently skipped.

3. **Honesty in completion.** "Completed all stages" should only print when stages genuinely ran or were verified-already-done, not when they were skipped without verification.

4. **Document the out-of-band recovery contract.** CLAUDE.md's "Manual finalize fallback" tells you how to run finalize by hand but doesn't say "and clear push's state after". That's a documentation gap too.

**Immediate practical path** (unchanged from my last message):
1. Add two new todos: (a) state-sanity preflight on resume, (b) stage-prereq verification + honest completion.
2. `vc-x1 push main --restart` to actually ship 0.37.2.

Green light for both?

> AGENT

**The probe worked — caught the culprit in one run.** Look at the delta:

```
track-probe enter vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked
...
track-probe exit  vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=NOT_TRACKED
```

`.claude(main)` went `tracked` → `NOT_TRACKED` **inside a single `vc-x1 push` invocation**. So the culprit is one of push's own stages, not an external event.

**Narrowing further** — push's parent process ran these stages against `.claude` before exiting:

1. `commit-claude`: `jj commit -R .claude`
2. `bookmark-both`: `jj bookmark set main -r @- -R .claude`
3. `finalize-claude`: spawned `vc-x1 finalize … --detach` (detached — parent doesn't wait; child has 10s delay before squash+push)

The 10s finalize delay means the detached child's squash+push hadn't started yet when the exit probe fired. So the tracking loss happened in stage 1 or 2 — almost certainly `jj commit` or `jj bookmark set`.

My bet: **`jj bookmark set main -r @- -R .claude`**. Moving a tracking bookmark to a commit that isn't a descendant of the tracked remote's tip can flag the bookmark as "moved away from its tracked remote" in jj, which some versions surface as dropping from the `--tracked` list even though the relationship still exists. Alternatively it could be `jj commit` if the working-copy's auto-advance interacts with the bookmark.

**Immediate data to confirm:**

```bash
jj op log -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude --limit 10
jj bookmark list --all-remotes -R /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/vc-x1/.claude
```

The op log will show the exact op that changed bookmark state; the bookmark listing will show current relationship (is `main@origin` still present? is local `main` ahead?).

Want me to run those probes? Or will you?

One side note on the push itself: the commit-and-push parent operations all ran, so this run **did** land the 0.37.2 commit on the app remote. `.claude`'s detached finalize will have pushed (or errored) by now. The main visible failure is the tracking-probe-captured regression, not the push itself.

> AGENT

Important plot twist — **I may have bugged the probe**, not caught a real regression. Look at the current state:

```
main: vqmlwnsr fa6e351e  ← local
  @git: vqmlwnsr fa6e351e
  @origin: vqmlwnsr fa6e351e  ← remote matches
```

Fully tracked, local and `@origin` at the same commit. That's clean state.

**But at the moment the exit probe fired**, the sequence was:
1. commit-claude made new commit `8fd3a69d…` / `07e3963d…` (id changed between ops, see op log)
2. bookmark-both moved local `main` to that new commit
3. **Probe fired here.** Local was at the new commit; `@origin` was still at the old (0.37.1) commit — local **ahead by one**.
4. Detached finalize's push caught `@origin` up; they rejoined.

My probe parses the output of `jj bookmark list --tracked main -R <repo>` and checks for a line starting with `@origin:`. But jj decorates the `@origin` line with sync state when local is ahead — something like:

```
  @origin (ahead by 1 commits): <old_commit>
```

That doesn't start with `@origin:` — it starts with `@origin (ahead…`. So my `starts_with("@origin:")` missed it. **False positive: probe reported NOT_TRACKED when tracking was actually fine, just ahead.**

This matches the op log: the local `jj bookmark set` + (later) `jj git push` sequence is exactly what creates the briefly-ahead-then-rejoined pattern. Tracking never actually dropped.

**Fix for the probe:** loosen the marker to `@origin` (no colon), or better yet query tracking status via jj's template language directly — something like `jj bookmark list --tracked main -T 'if(self.remote(), "tracked", "not-tracked")'` — instead of parsing human-readable output.

Small follow-up edit. Want me to fix the probe and re-test, so we can tell whether there's a real tracking-loss bug hiding anywhere, or if every "NOT_TRACKED" we've seen from the probe was just my parsing glitch?

> AGENT

Second probe bug caught by the same probe — and it's mine, not jj's.

You ran from inside `.claude`. My probe uses hardcoded relative paths `.` and `.claude`:

- `.` from inside `.claude` → `/…/vc-x1/.claude` (HAS `.jj`, so labeled "app(main)=tracked" — but that's actually `.claude`'s tracking being reported as "app")
- `.claude` from inside `.claude` → `/…/vc-x1/.claude/.claude` (doesn't exist → `no-jj`)

So both labels are lying depending on cwd. Probe needs to find the **workspace root** (walking up looking for `.vc-config.toml` or similar) before probing, not blindly use cwd-relative paths.

So the probe has **two bugs** to fix:

1. Strict `@origin:` marker parse misses the "local-ahead" decorated form.
2. cwd-relative paths mislabel and miss when cwd isn't the workspace root.

Let me find the workspace-root helper and fix both.

221	/// TEMPORARY — diagnostic probe for the non-tracking-bookmark issue
222	/// surfaced during the 0.37.1 dogfood. Prints `main` bookmark tracking
223	/// status in both repos of the dual-repo workspace on entry and exit
224	/// of every command. If entry and exit differ, the executing command
225	/// is the culprit; if entry is `NOT_TRACKED` but a previous command's
226	/// exit was `tracked`, something *between* invocations broke it.
227	/// Rip out once the culprit is identified — search for "track-probe".
228	fn track_probe(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {
229	    let repos: [(&Path, &str); 2] = [(Path::new("."), "app"), (Path::new(".claude"), ".claude")];
230	    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
231	    for (repo, label) in repos {
232	        if !repo.join(".jj").exists() {
233	            parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=no-jj"));
234	            continue;
235	        }
236	        match track_probe_one(repo, "main", "origin") {
237	            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=tracked")),
238	            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!("{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED")),
239	            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(
240	                "{label}(main)=err({})",
241	                e.lines().next().unwrap_or("")
242	            )),
243	        }
244	    }
245	    log::info!(
246	        "track-probe {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}: {}",
247	        parts.join(", ")
248	    );
249	}
250	
251	/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.
252	/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` names
253	/// `@<remote>`, `Ok(false)` if the bookmark isn't tracking that
254	/// remote (or doesn't exist), `Err` on subprocess failure.
255	fn track_probe_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {
256	    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();
257	    let output = std::process::Command::new("jj")
258	        .args(["bookmark", "list", "--tracked", bookmark, "-R", &repo_str])
259	        .output()
260	        .map_err(|e| format!("spawn: {e}"))?;
261	    if !output.status.success() {
262	        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());
263	    }
264	    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
265	    let marker = format!("@{remote}:");
266	    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(&marker)))
267	}
268	
269	fn main() -> ExitCode {
270	    CompleteEnv::with_factory(cli_with_banner).complete();
271	    let matches = cli_with_banner().get_matches();
272	    let cli = match Cli::from_arg_matches(&matches) {
273	        Ok(cli) => cli,
274	        Err(e) => {
275	            e.exit();
276	        }
277	    };
278	
279	    let log_path = cli.log.as_ref().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().to_string());
280	    logging::CliLogger::init(cli.verbose, log_path.as_deref());
281	
282	    // The detached `finalize --exec` re-entry is the bot's
283	    // session-end child; it shouldn't print the banner (extra
284	    // chatter in its log) or surface user-facing failure markers
285	    // (those are meant for the user's next interactive run).
286	    let is_detached_exec = matches!(
287	        cli.command,
288	        Commands::Finalize(ref f) if f.exec
289	    );
290	
291	    // `-L` / `--no-label` on the read-only multi-repo subcommands
292	    // (chid, desc, list, show) makes their output script-parseable
293	    // — adding a leading `vc-x1 X.Y.Z` line would break that. Skip
294	    // the banner whenever the active subcommand has the flag set.
295	    let suppress_banner = match &cli.command {
296	        Commands::Chid(a) => a.common.no_label,
297	        Commands::Desc(a) => a.common.no_label,
298	        Commands::List(a) => a.common.no_label,

  "new_string": "/// TEMPORARY — diagnostic probe for the non-tracking-bookmark issue\n/// surfaced during the 0.37.1 dogfood. Prints `main` bookmark tracking\n/// status in both repos of the dual-repo workspace on entry and exit\n/// of every command. If entry and exit differ, the executing command\n/// is the culprit; if entry is `NOT_TRACKED` but a previous command's\n/// exit was `tracked`, something *between* invocations broke it.\n/// Rip out once the culprit is identified — search for \"track-probe\".\n///\n/// Walks up from cwd to locate the workspace root (the directory whose\n/// `.vc-config.toml` has `path = \"/\"`), then probes `<root>` and\n/// `<root>/.claude`. So the probe gives the same labeling whether the\n/// user runs from the app root, from `.claude`, or from any subdir.\nfn track_probe(phase: &str, command_name: &str) {\n    let header = format!(\"track-probe {phase} vc-x1 {command_name}\");\n    let root = match find_workspace_root() {\n        Some(r) => r,\n        None => {\n            log::info!(\"{header}: no-workspace\");\n            return;\n        }\n    };\n    let repos: [(std::path::PathBuf, &str); 2] = [\n        (root.clone(), \"app\"),\n        (root.join(\".claude\"), \".claude\"),\n    ];\n    let mut parts: Vec<String> = Vec::new();\n    for (repo, label) in repos {\n        if !repo.join(\".jj\").exists() {\n            parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=no-jj\"));\n            continue;\n        }\n        match track_probe_one(&repo, \"main\", \"origin\") {\n            Ok(true) => parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=tracked\")),\n            Ok(false) => parts.push(format!(\"{label}(main)=NOT_TRACKED\")),\n            Err(e) => parts.push(format!(\n                \"{label}(main)=err({})\",\n                e.lines().next().unwrap_or(\"\")\n            )),\n        }\n    }\n    log::info!(\"{header}: {}\", parts.join(\", \"));\n}\n\n/// Walk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = \"/\"`.\n/// That's the workspace root — the app-repo side of the dual-repo\n/// pair. Returns `None` if no such config is found up to filesystem\n/// root (e.g., running from outside any vc-x1 workspace).\nfn find_workspace_root() -> Option<std::path::PathBuf> {\n    let mut cur = std::env::current_dir().ok()?;\n    loop {\n        let config = cur.join(\".vc-config.toml\");\n        if let Ok(content) = std::fs::read_to_string(&config) {\n            // Match either `path = \"/\"` or `path=\"/\"` (with/without spaces).\n            let compact: String = content\n                .lines()\n                .find(|l| l.trim_start().starts_with(\"path\"))\n                .unwrap_or(\"\")\n                .chars()\n                .filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace())\n                .collect();\n            if compact.starts_with(\"path=\\\"/\\\"\") {\n                return Some(cur);\n            }\n        }\n        cur = cur.parent()?.to_path_buf();\n    }\n}\n\n/// Query jj for whether `bookmark` in `repo` is tracking `remote`.\n/// Returns `Ok(true)` if the tracked-list entry for `bookmark` shows\n/// an `@<remote>` line (in any form — `@origin:` when synced, or\n/// `@origin (ahead by N commits):` / similar when divergent — both\n/// still count as tracking). `Ok(false)` when no such line exists\n/// (bookmark isn't tracking this remote or doesn't exist). `Err` on\n/// subprocess failure.\nfn track_probe_one(repo: &Path, bookmark: &str, remote: &str) -> Result<bool, String> {\n    let repo_str = repo.to_string_lossy();\n    let output = std::process::Command::new(\"jj\")\n        .args([\"bookmark\", \"list\", \"--tracked\", bookmark, \"-R\", &repo_str])\n        .output()\n        .map_err(|e| format!(\"spawn: {e}\"))?;\n    if !output.status.success() {\n        return Err(String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr).trim().to_string());\n    }\n    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);\n    // Accept both the synced form (`@origin:`) and the decorated\n    // divergent form (`@origin (ahead by N commits):`), which still\n    // represents a tracking relationship.\n    let colon = format!(\"@{remote}:\");\n    let paren = format!(\"@{remote} \");\n    Ok(stdout.lines().any(|l| {\n        let t = l.trim_start();\n        t.starts_with(&colon) || t.starts_with(&paren)\n    }))\n}"
}

Run\nfrom inside `.claude`, those resolve to `.claude` and `.claude/.claude`\nrespectively — probe reported `app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=no-jj`,\nwhich is doubly wrong: the \"app\" column is actually showing `.claude`'s\nstatus, and the \".claude\" column is showing a nonexistent path. Fix:\nwalk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = \"/\"`.\nThat file marks the app-repo side of the dual-repo pair, so its\ncontaining dir is the workspace root. Probe `<root>` and\n`<root>/.claude` unconditionally — gives consistent labeling from\nany cwd inside the workspace. Outside any workspace, probe prints\n`no-workspace` and skips.\n\n**Bug B — `@origin:` parse misses the divergent-decorated form.**\nProbe checked `line.trim_start().starts_with(\"@origin:\")`. But when\nlocal `main` is ahead of `@origin`, jj decorates the line as\n`@origin (ahead by 1 commits): <old_commit>` — same tracking\nrelationship, different prefix, my check fails. **This is what\nproduced the scary-looking `NOT_TRACKED` at 0.37.2's push-exit\nprobe** — the bookmark WAS tracking, it was just briefly\nlocal-ahead between `bookmark-both` and the detached finalize's\npush. False positive. Fix: accept both `@origin:` (synced) and\n`@origin ` (divergent-decorated, note trailing space) as tracking\nmarkers. The tracking relationship is what matters; sync-state is\northogonal.\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `track_probe` rewritten to call\n  `find_workspace_root()` first, then probe `<root>` + `<root>/.claude`.\n  Graceful fallback to `no-workspace` when cwd is outside any vc-x1\n  workspace.\n- `src/main.rs` — new `find_workspace_root()` helper. Walks up from\n  cwd reading each `.vc-config.toml` it finds; matches if the file\n  has `path = \"/\"` (with or without surrounding whitespace). Returns\n  `None` at filesystem root if no match found.\n- `src/main.rs` — `track_probe_one` tracking detection loosened.\n  Now accepts either `@{remote}:` or `@{remote} ` (trailing space\n  for the decorated form). Doc comment expanded to call out both\n  cases.\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.2` → `0.37.3`.\n\nImplication for the chain of diagnoses: the `NOT_TRACKED` seen at\n0.37.2's push-exit probe was a false positive. There is no evidence\nyet of an actual tracking-loss bug inside vc-x1's push flow. The\n*original* tracking loss that triggered the 0.37.1 finalize-claude\nfailure was real (`jj git push` genuinely refused), but that loss\nhappened before any probe existed — origin lies somewhere in\n`.claude`'s historical bootstrap, not in the recent commit+push\nsequence. The probe continues to provide value going forward: it\nwould catch any new tracking-loss the moment it happens."
}

All green. Proposed commit:

**Title** (35 chars):

```
fix: tracking-probe bugs (0.37.3)

**Body**:

```
First use of 0.37.2's tracking probe caught two bugs — in the
probe itself, not in vc-x1's push flow. Notable corollary: the
scary `NOT_TRACKED` at 0.37.2 push exit was a false positive
(Bug B below), not a real tracking-loss regression.

Bug A — cwd-relative paths mislabel and miss. Hardcoded `.` and
`.claude` as repo paths; run from inside `.claude` they
resolved wrong. Fix: walk up from cwd for `.vc-config.toml`
with `path = "/"` and probe <root> + <root>/.claude.

Bug B — `@origin:` parse misses divergent-decorated form. When
local is ahead, jj writes `@origin (ahead by N commits):`; my
starts_with("@origin:") missed it. Fix: accept both the synced
form and the decorated form as tracking markers.

- src/main.rs: track_probe rewritten to use find_workspace_root();
  new find_workspace_root helper (walks up, reads .vc-config.toml,
  matches `path = "/"`); track_probe_one tracking check loosened
  to accept `@origin:` and `@origin ` prefixes
- Cargo.toml / Cargo.lock: 0.37.2 → 0.37.3
- notes/chores-05.md: new 0.37.3 subsection
- notes/todo.md: Done line + ref [56]
```

> DEVELOPER

So both bug A and B were operator errors in some sense and we haven't really found "the" problem?

> AGENT

You're exactly right, and it's worth saying out loud: **we still haven't identified what broke `.claude`'s tracking in the first place.**

What we actually have:

- **Real recovery.** The 0.37.1 push failure was genuine — `jj git push` refused because tracking was missing on this workspace. We fixed it with `jj bookmark track`.
- **Real diagnosis of the structural truth.** Tracking is per-workspace, not shared via git refs. This is useful system knowledge but it's not *why* tracking was missing on this particular workspace.
- **Two probe bugs fixed** — both mine. Zero evidence of vc-x1 itself breaking tracking captured by the (now-correct) probe.

What we **don't** have:

- A reproducible trigger.
- A confirmed commit/operation where tracking was dropped.
- Any idea whether the bug is historical (one-time setup mishap that's long since healed) or latent (waiting for specific conditions).

Two likely profiles for the original loss, in descending likelihood:

1. **Historical artifact.** `.claude` on 3900x was bootstrapped at some past point — possibly before `init.rs:482-485` ever added the `jj bookmark track` call, possibly by an ad-hoc manual `jj git init --colocate` + `jj git fetch`, possibly by an old `vc-x1 init`. That one-time miss persisted until we pushed from this machine for the first time. Op log's oldest entry (23 hours ago) doesn't go back far enough to confirm.

2. **Latent bug we haven't triggered.** Some specific vc-x1 operation (or a specific state under which it runs) drops tracking, and we just haven't repeated the conditions. The now-correct probe would catch this, but nothing we've done since has caught it.

Options from here:

- **Leave the probe in through more dogfood**, hope it catches a repeat. Low cost; requires patience and a repro.
- **Rip the probe now** (we haven't caught anything in several clean runs), accept the original loss as historical, and rely on the "detect-and-error in sync/push preflight" todo as the real protection against any future recurrence.
- **Actively try to reproduce**: bootstrap a fresh `.claude` via various setup paths (historical init, `jj git init --colocate` + fetch, etc.) and see which produces a non-tracking bookmark by default. If one does, that's probably what happened here.

My lean: option 3 as a small follow-up (hour or two of systematic testing), then either the probe catches something or we retire it with a clear "historical — can't reproduce" note and move on. No point keeping a diagnostic in place indefinitely when we've had multiple clean runs.

Your call — happy to leave it, rip it, or try to reproduce. What's the cadence you want?

> DEVELOPER

I've had it happen more than once I suggest we keep the entry/exit checks in, I don't think they can hurt us, IMO

> AGENT

Agree it's worth keeping — "happens more than once" is the key data point I didn't have. The probe's cheap and the signal is valuable. Two refinements to consider though:

**1. Promote from "temporary" to "permanent" in docs.** The 0.37.2 chores entry frames it as temporary with a ripout checklist. If we're keeping it, that framing is misleading for future readers. Small doc edit: reframe as a permanent sanity check, drop the ripout section.

**2. Quieter when clean?** Right now the probe outputs *two* lines per command regardless of state:

```
track-probe enter vc-x1 chid: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked
track-probe exit  vc-x1 chid: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked
```

Every command, every time. After what we did in 0.37.1 to quiet subprocess output, this stands out. Alternative behavior:

- **Silent when clean** — always probe both entry and exit, but only *print* when:
  (a) entry reports anything other than "all tracked", OR
  (b) exit differs from entry (the probe catches a change)

  Net effect: clean runs stay silent (zero lines), problem runs are loud (loud error-level line naming the command that caused the change).

- Keep always-on output (your current suggestion) — full transparency, two lines/command forever.

My lean: **silent-when-clean preserves the full detection value and removes the steady noise**. The probe fires on every command; the output only appears when there's something to say. That's the pattern most UNIX-y tools converge on ("diff of nothing is nothing").

But this is your system and your signal preference. Happy either way. Want:

- (a) Keep both bugs fixed as-is from 0.37.3 (verbose every command) + tiny doc reframing in a 0.37.4, or
- (b) Refine to silent-when-clean + doc reframing together in 0.37.4?

> DEVELOPER

I vote keeping it as is for now and gain confidence that the "signal" is solid.

> AGENT

Good discipline — don't touch the signal while you're still calibrating trust in it.

So 0.37.4 is a tiny doc-only reframing:

- `src/main.rs` — `track_probe` doc comment: drop "TEMPORARY" and "Rip out once the culprit is identified". Replace with a neutral "permanent sanity check; stays verbose until signal confidence is established, then consider quieter-when-clean refinement" note.
- `notes/chores-05.md` — new 0.37.4 subsection that amends 0.37.2's "temporary diagnostic" framing to "permanent (pending quiet-mode refinement)". Note the "happened more than once" data point as the reason to keep it.
- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.3` → `0.37.4`.
- `notes/todo.md` — Done line + new Todo entry for the silent-when-clean refinement (captured but deferred).

No code behavior change. Pre-commit checklist should be trivial. Green light?

sg, but just to be clear you haven't made any changes yet, correct?

The probe continues to provide value going forward: it\nwould catch any new tracking-loss the moment it happens.\n\n## Promote track-probe from temporary to permanent (0.37.4)\n\nUser data point after the 0.37.3 bug fixes: \"I've had it happen\nmore than once.\" That reframes the probe from \"one-shot\ninvestigation\" (add, catch, rip out) to \"permanent sanity check\"\n(always on, signals a real-but-rare class of failures).\n\nAmendment to the 0.37.2 + 0.37.3 framing, not code behavior —\nthe probe itself is unchanged, only its docs and status.\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `track_probe` doc comment rewritten. Drops\n  \"TEMPORARY\" and the \"Rip out once the culprit is identified\"\n  line. Adds context on why it stayed in place (multiple past\n  occurrences reported) and notes the deferred \"silent when\n  clean\" refinement that would preserve detection value while\n  removing steady-state noise.\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.3` → `0.37.4`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — Done line + new Todo for the quieter-mode\n  refinement (silent when clean, loud on mismatch).\n\nThe ripout checklist in 0.37.2's subsection and the references\nto \"temporary\" in 0.37.3's subsection are preserved as historical\nrecord rather than edited in place — they accurately describe\nthe authors' intent *at the time those versions shipped*, and\nrewriting them would erase the story of how the decision\nevolved. Future readers find the authoritative status here\n(subsection 0.37.4) via the normal chronological read."
}
```

**Body**:

```
User data point after 0.37.3: "I've had it happen more than
once." That reframes the probe from one-shot investigation
to permanent sanity check. Docs only; no code behavior change.

- src/main.rs: track_probe doc comment drops "TEMPORARY" and
  "Rip out once the culprit is identified". Adds context on
  why it's permanent (multiple past occurrences) and notes
  the deferred "silent when clean" refinement.
- Cargo.toml / Cargo.lock: 0.37.3 → 0.37.4
- notes/chores-05.md: new 0.37.4 subsection amending the
  0.37.2 + 0.37.3 "temporary" framing. Historical subsections
  left intact as a record of how intent evolved.
- notes/todo.md: Done line + new Todo for the silent-when-clean
  refinement (deferred until signal-confidence is established)
```

OK to run `vc-x1 push main --yes ...`?

> DEVELOPER

We'll be removing the always on entry/exit after we can confidence and it'll be failure only in the near future, or are you talking about something else?

> AGENT

Same thing, yes — I called it "silent when clean" but that's identical to your "failure only". Behavior intent:

- **Now (0.37.4)**: always print entry + exit. No change from 0.37.3.
- **Later (some 0.37.N)**: always probe, but only print on `NOT_TRACKED` or `entry != exit`. Silent in the common clean case.

The todo entry captures that, and your earlier "keep as-is for now and gain confidence" is what gates the transition — we'll refine once you trust the signal.

So 0.37.4 is *just* the doc reframing (promote from temporary to permanent, drop the ripout-checklist language). No behavior change. Same proposed commit as above.

OK to push?

> DEVELOPER

Ready to push, or any other thought before we commit?

> DEVELOPER

Sorry, why do you call it "track-probe"

> AGENT

It's just the log-prefix string I picked when I sketched the probe earlier — "track" for bookmark tracking, "probe" as in diagnostic instrument. It's arbitrary naming. I chose it for:

- Short (one word-pair), visible at a glance in output
- Specific enough to grep for (`grep track-probe` or `grep -v track-probe`)
- Says "this is telling you about tracking"
- Says "this is an inserted observer, not part of the actual work"

Alternatives if you prefer:

- `tracking:` — simpler, but ambiguous with other "tracking" concepts
- `track-check:` — emphasizes verify-step rather than observer role
- `bookmark-track:` — most explicit, longest
- `probe:` — generic, loses the domain info

Happy to rename to any of those or something you like better. It's a prefix string sprinkled through `main.rs` + `notes/chores-05.md`; replace-all is a two-line edit. Want to swap it?

> DEVELOPER

We use TProbe (Time Probe) for our actor-x1 project so it's not a great name.

> AGENT

Fair point. Let me offer a few alternatives, aiming for "short, domain-specific, unambiguous":

- `tracking:` — simplest, says what it's about. Slight risk of colliding with the English word in some unrelated log line, but I don't see any in vc-x1's output today.
- `track-check:` — adds the verb. Clearer that it's a check, not a general status.
- `track-status:` — descriptive, slightly longer.
- `bm-track:` — explicitly names the bookmark concept. Short.

My lean: **`track-check:`** — one more character than `track-probe:` but reads as what it does ("we're checking tracking state"), doesn't collide with TProbe or the English word, and stays greppable.

Since 0.37.4 is still pre-commit and unrelated to the rename, two options:

1. **Fold rename into 0.37.4** — grep + replace-all `track-probe` → `track-check` across `src/main.rs` and `notes/chores-05.md`. One-line additional scope, same commit.
2. **Rename in a follow-up 0.37.5** — keeps 0.37.4 scope pure.

Your pick on the name AND the folding decision.

> DEVELOPER

I vote bm-track

> AGENT

Going with `bm-track`, folded into 0.37.4 (small edit, still pre-commit). Flag me if you'd rather split.

> AGENT

Smoke test looks right — `bm-track` prefix, both tracked.

**On the interruption question**: Claude Code's built-in way to interrupt me mid-tool-call is **Esc** (once cancels the current tool call and gives you the prompt; the typed message gets read as my next turn). Anything you type while I'm working queues up and gets delivered when the current turn finishes — that's how your `/btw` pattern works.

There's no built-in `/pause` or `/stop` slash command. Two options if you want explicit ones:

1. **Just use Esc as "/stop"** — it's the terminal-native interrupt. One key, always available, cancels cleanly.
2. **Define a custom slash skill** — a `/pause` skill in `~/.claude/skills/pause/SKILL.md` that just says "stop and wait for the user" would give you a typed command that does the same thing. Happy to help you set that up separately.

For quick "hold on" moments during a long task, Esc is the fastest path. For "stop this specific line of thought and talk first", queuing a text message (no slash needed — just type) is usually enough since I read it at the next safe point.

to avoid collision with actor-x1's TProbe (time probe).

765	## Temporary bookmark-tracking diagnostic probe (0.37.2)
766	
767	0.37.1's first-dogfood push hit a `bookmark 'main' has non-tracking
768	remote 'main@origin'` error in the `.claude` repo during the
769	`finalize-claude` stage. Recovery was one-time: `jj bookmark track
770	main --remote=origin -R .claude`. But the open question is *when*
771	and *why* the tracking flag got lost — the fwlaptop op log shows
772	`.claude` pushing fine from there, so the 3900x workspace ended
773	up in a different state at some historical point.
774	
775	The detect-and-error work (`notes/todo.md` top entry) is the
776	long-term policy fix. This chore is a **temporary diagnostic**
777	to localize the culprit: probe `main`-bookmark tracking status on
778	entry and exit of every vc-x1 command. If entry and exit differ
779	during a single command, that command is the culprit; if entry
780	differs from the previous command's exit, something *between*
781	invocations broke it (external tooling, manual jj commands, etc.).
782	
783	Output shape:
784	
785	```
786	bm-track enter vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=tracked
787	bm-track exit  vc-x1 push: app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=NOT_TRACKED
788	```
789	
790	- `src/main.rs` — `use std::path::Path;` added. Two new functions:
791	  `bm_track(phase, command_name)` prints the status line for
792	  both repos; `bm_track_one(repo, bookmark, remote)` queries
793	  jj via `jj bookmark list --tracked <bookmark> -R <repo>`. The
794	  tracked-list entry includes a line starting with `@<remote>:`
795	  when the bookmark is tracking that remote; absence means
796	  not-tracking.
797	- `src/main.rs` — dispatch block wraps the `match cli.command`
798	  in a `let exit_code = match ...;` binding, with `bm_track`
799	  calls immediately before and after. Skipped when `is_detached_exec`
800	  (the `finalize --exec` re-entry) since probe noise in that log
801	  isn't useful.
802	- Graceful degradation — if the repo doesn't exist, missing-`.jj/`
803	  detection emits `no-jj`; subprocess failure emits `err(<first line>)`.
804	  Never blocks the wrapped command.
805	- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.1` → `0.37.2`.
806	
807	**How to rip it out** once the culprit is identified:
808	
809	```bash
810	grep -rn 'bm-track\|bm_track' src/
811	```
812	
813	Three places to touch:
814	1. `src/main.rs` — delete the two functions (`bm_track`,
815	   `bm_track_one`) and the `use std::path::Path` import if
816	   nothing else needs it.
817	2. `src/main.rs` — revert the dispatch to plain
818	   `match cli.command { ... }` (drop the `let exit_code = ...`
819	   binding, the two `bm_track(...)` calls, and the
820	   `command_name` binding).
821	3. `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — bump to the next appropriate
822	   version.
823	
824	Net removal: ~80 lines.
825	
826	## Fix two probe bugs (0.37.3)
827	
828	First use of the 0.37.2 probe caught two bugs — in the probe itself,
829	not in vc-x1's push flow.
```

828	
829	## Fix two probe bugs (0.37.3)
830	
831	First use of the 0.37.2 probe caught two bugs — in the probe itself,
832	not in vc-x1's push flow.
833	
834	**Bug A — cwd-relative paths mislabel and miss.** The probe hardcoded
835	`Path::new(".")` and `Path::new(".claude")` as the repo paths. Run
836	from inside `.claude`, those resolve to `.claude` and `.claude/.claude`
837	respectively — probe reported `app(main)=tracked, .claude(main)=no-jj`,
838	which is doubly wrong: the "app" column is actually showing `.claude`'s
839	status, and the ".claude" column is showing a nonexistent path. Fix:
840	walk up from cwd looking for `.vc-config.toml` with `path = "/"`.
841	That file marks the app-repo side of the dual-repo pair, so its
842	containing dir is the workspace root. Probe `<root>` and
843	`<root>/.claude` unconditionally — gives consistent labeling from
844	any cwd inside the workspace. Outside any workspace, probe prints
845	`no-workspace` and skips.
846	
847	**Bug B — `@origin:` parse misses the divergent-decorated form.**
848	Probe checked `line.trim_start().starts_with("@origin:")`. But when
849	local `main` is ahead of `@origin`, jj decorates the line as
850	`@origin (ahead by 1 commits): <old_commit>` — same tracking
851	relationship, different prefix, my check fails. **This is what
852	produced the scary-looking `NOT_TRACKED` at 0.37.2's push-exit
853	probe** — the bookmark WAS tracking, it was just briefly
854	local-ahead between `bookmark-both` and the detached finalize's
855	push. False positive. Fix: accept both `@origin:` (synced) and
856	`@origin ` (divergent-decorated, note trailing space) as tracking
857	markers. The tracking relationship is what matters; sync-state is
858	orthogonal.
859	
860	- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track` rewritten to call
861	  `find_workspace_root()` first, then probe `<root>` + `<root>/.claude`.
862	  Graceful fallback to `no-workspace` when cwd is outside any vc-x1
863	  workspace.
864	- `src/main.rs` — new `find_workspace_root()` helper. Walks up from
865	  cwd reading each `.vc-config.toml` it finds; matches if the file
866	  has `path = "/"` (with or without surrounding whitespace). Returns
867	  `None` at filesystem root if no match found.
868	- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track_one` tracking detection loosened.
869	  Now accepts either `@{remote}:` or `@{remote} ` (trailing space
870	  for the decorated form). Doc comment expanded to call out both
871	  cases.
872	- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.2` → `0.37.3`.
873	
874	Implication for the chain of diagnoses: the `NOT_TRACKED` seen at
875	0.37.2's push-exit probe was a false positive. There is no evidence
876	yet of an actual tracking-loss bug inside vc-x1's push flow. The
877	*original* tracking loss that triggered the 0.37.1 finalize-claude
878	failure was real (`jj git push` genuinely refused), but that loss
879	happened before any probe existed — origin lies somewhere in
880	`.claude`'s historical bootstrap, not in the recent commit+push
881	sequence. The probe continues to provide value going forward: it
882	would catch any new tracking-loss the moment it happens.
883	
884	## Promote bm-track from temporary to permanent + rename (0.37.4)
885	
886	User data point after the 0.37.3 bug fixes: "I've had it happen
887	more than once." That reframes the probe from "one-shot
888	investigation" (add, catch, rip out) to "permanent sanity check"
889	(always on, signals a real-but-rare class of failures).
890	
891	Also picked a less-collision-prone log prefix: original
892	`track-probe` overlaps with the actor-x1 sibling project's
893	"TProbe" (time probe) naming. Renamed to `bm-track` (short for
894	"bookmark tracking") — shorter, domain-specific, no collision.
895	
896	Amendment to the 0.37.2 + 0.37.3 framing, not behavioral —
897	the probe's logic is unchanged, only its docs, status, and
898	identifier.
899	
900	- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track` doc comment rewritten. Drops
901	  "TEMPORARY" and the "Rip out once the culprit is identified"
902	  line. Adds context on why it stayed in place (multiple past
903	  occurrences reported) and notes the deferred "silent when
904	  clean" refinement that would preserve detection value while
905	  removing steady-state noise.
906	- `src/main.rs` — functions and log prefix renamed:
907	  `track_probe` → `bm_track`, `track_probe_one` → `bm_track_one`,
908	  log prefix `track-probe` → `bm-track`. Greppable, consistent.
909	- `notes/chores-05.md` — earlier 0.37.2 + 0.37.3 subsections
910	  updated in place for the rename (they reference code symbols;
911	  those symbols have new names as of this commit). The
912	  "temporary" narrative framing in those subsections is
913	  preserved — that's the story of how intent evolved and
914	  rewriting it would erase the reasoning trail.
915	- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.3` → `0.37.4`.
916	- `notes/todo.md` — Done line + new Todo for the quieter-mode
917	  refinement (silent when clean, loud on mismatch; entry state
918	  carried into the exit-line printing so the transition is
919	  evidence, not inference).
920	
```

The probe continues to provide value going forward: it\nwould catch any new tracking-loss the moment it happens.\n\n## Promote bm-track from temporary to permanent + rename (0.37.4)\n\nUser data point after the 0.37.3 bug fixes: \"I've had it happen\nmore than once.\" That reframes the probe from \"one-shot\ninvestigation\" (add, catch, rip out) to \"permanent sanity check\"\n(always on, signals a real-but-rare class of failures).\n\nAlso picked a less-collision-prone log prefix: original\n`track-probe` overlaps with the actor-x1 sibling project's\n\"TProbe\" (time probe) naming. Renamed to `bm-track` (short for\n\"bookmark tracking\") — shorter, domain-specific, no collision.\n\nAmendment to the 0.37.2 + 0.37.3 framing, not behavioral —\nthe probe's logic is unchanged, only its docs, status, and\nidentifier.\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track` doc comment rewritten. Drops\n  \"TEMPORARY\" and the \"Rip out once the culprit is identified\"\n  line. Adds context on why it stayed in place (multiple past\n  occurrences reported) and notes the deferred \"silent when\n  clean\" refinement that would preserve detection value while\n  removing steady-state noise.\n- `src/main.rs` — functions and log prefix renamed:\n  `track_probe` → `bm_track`, `track_probe_one` → `bm_track_one`,\n  log prefix `track-probe` → `bm-track`. Greppable, consistent.\n- `notes/chores-05.md` — earlier 0.37.2 + 0.37.3 subsections\n  updated in place for the rename (they reference code symbols;\n  those symbols have new names as of this commit). The\n  \"temporary\" narrative framing in those subsections is\n  preserved — that's the story of how intent evolved and\n  rewriting it would erase the reasoning trail.\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.3` → `0.37.4`.\n- `notes/todo.md` — Done line + new Todo for the quieter-mode\n  refinement (silent when clean, loud on mismatch; entry state\n  carried into the exit-line printing so the transition is\n  evidence, not inference).",
  "new_string": "## Fix bm-track bugs + rename + promote to permanent (0.37.3)\n\nThree concerns bundled into this patch — all tied to the\nbookmark-tracking probe added in 0.37.2:\n\n1. **Two bugs in the probe itself** (the original motivation for\n   0.37.3). First real use in 0.37.2 caught them — not vc-x1's\n   push flow, as the output had misleadingly suggested.\n2. **Rename** from `track-probe` / `track_probe*` to `bm-track` /\n   `bm_track*` to avoid collision with the actor-x1 sibling\n   project's \"TProbe\" (time probe) naming.\n3. **Promote from temporary to permanent.** User data point:\n   \"I've had it happen more than once.\" Drops the 0.37.2\n   ripout-checklist framing in favor of a stays-in-place sanity\n   check.\n\n### Bug A — cwd-relative paths mislabel and miss\n\nThe 0.37.2 probe hardcoded `Path::new(\".\")` and\n`Path::new(\".claude\")` as the repo paths. Run from inside\n`.claude`, those resolve to `.claude` and `.claude/.claude`\nrespectively — probe reported `app(main)=tracked,\n.claude(main)=no-jj`, which is doubly wrong: the \"app\" column\nis actually showing `.claude`'s status, and the \".claude\" column\nis showing a nonexistent path. Fix: walk up from cwd looking for\n`.vc-config.toml` with `path = \"/\"`. That file marks the app-repo\nside of the dual-repo pair, so its containing dir is the\nworkspace root. Probe `<root>` and `<root>/.claude` unconditionally\n— gives consistent labeling from any cwd inside the workspace.\nOutside any workspace, probe prints `no-workspace` and skips.\n\n### Bug B — `@origin:` parse misses the divergent-decorated form\n\nThe 0.37.2 probe checked `line.trim_start().starts_with(\"@origin:\")`.\nBut when local `main` is ahead of `@origin`, jj decorates the line\nas `@origin (ahead by 1 commits): <old_commit>` — same tracking\nrelationship, different prefix, the check fails. **This is what\nproduced the scary-looking `NOT_TRACKED` at 0.37.2's push-exit\nprobe** — the bookmark WAS tracking, it was just briefly\nlocal-ahead between `bookmark-both` and the detached finalize's\npush. False positive. Fix: accept both `@origin:` (synced) and\n`@origin ` (divergent-decorated, trailing space) as tracking\nmarkers. The tracking relationship is what matters; sync-state is\northogonal.\n\n### Rename\n\nLog prefix `track-probe` in 0.37.2's output collides conceptually\nwith actor-x1's \"TProbe\" (time probe). Renamed here to `bm-track`\n(short for \"bookmark tracking\") — shorter, domain-specific, no\ncollision. Grep-replaced across source and the current chores\nentry; the 0.37.2 subsection above was left using the original\n`track-probe` name for historical accuracy at that shipping point.\n\n### Promote to permanent\n\n0.37.2 framed the probe as temporary with a ripout checklist.\nUser confirmed \"happens more than once\" — i.e., the tracking-loss\nclass of failures is rare but real, and a permanent always-on\nsanity check beats waiting to rip the probe out after one catch.\nProbe logic unchanged; only its doc comment and the 0.37.2\nripout-checklist framing are retired (the checklist is left in\nthe 0.37.2 subsection as historical record, annotated as\nsuperseded here).\n\n### Edits\n\n- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track` rewritten to call\n  `find_workspace_root()` first, then probe `<root>` +\n  `<root>/.claude`. Graceful fallback to `no-workspace` when cwd\n  is outside any vc-x1 workspace.\n- `src/main.rs` — new `find_workspace_root()` helper. Walks up\n  from cwd reading each `.vc-config.toml` it finds; matches if\n  the file has `path = \"/\"` (with or without surrounding\n  whitespace). Returns `None` at filesystem root if no match\n  found.\n- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track_one` tracking detection loosened.\n  Now accepts either `@{remote}:` or `@{remote} ` (trailing\n  space for the decorated form). Doc comment expanded to call\n  out both cases.\n- `src/main.rs` — functions and log prefix renamed:\n  `track_probe` → `bm_track`, `track_probe_one` →\n  `bm_track_one`, log prefix `track-probe` → `bm-track`.\n- `src/main.rs` — `bm_track` doc comment drops \"TEMPORARY\" and\n  the \"Rip out once the culprit is identified\" line. Adds context\n  on why it stays in place (multiple past occurrences reported)\n  and notes the deferred \"silent when clean\" refinement that\n  would preserve detection value while removing steady-state\n  noise (see `notes/todo.md`).\n- `Cargo.toml` / `Cargo.lock` — `0.37.2` → `0.37.3`.\n\nImplication for the chain of diagnoses: the `NOT_TRACKED` seen\nat 0.37.2's push-exit probe was a false positive from Bug B.\nThere is no evidence yet of an actual tracking-loss bug inside\nvc-x1's push flow. The *original* tracking loss that triggered\nthe 0.37.1 finalize-claude failure was real (`jj git push`\ngenuinely refused), but that loss happened before any probe\nexisted — origin lies somewhere in `.claude`'s historical\nbootstrap, not in the recent commit+push sequence. The probe\n(now permanent) continues to provide value going forward: it\nwould catch any new tracking-loss the moment it happens."
}

> DEVELOPER

We could do both at once with `vc-x1 push` with the right flags? Let me word it differently soon I want `vc-x1 push` to be able to operate on either repo singularly or both as it does now. Also this entire process can be made to work with `vc-x1 push` if/when we support squashing on code repo. So should this work and is it worth the effort in you opinion?

> AGENT

Yes to all three. Breaking down the honest opinion:

**1. "Could `vc-x1 push` do both force-pushes at once with the right flags?"** — Yes. Today's design is close; it just needs two new flags:

- **`--scope=app|claude|both`** (default `both`, matches current behavior) for single-repo operation.
- **`--amend`** (or `--squash-into-last`) to fold WC into `@-` via `--ignore-immutable` instead of creating a new commit. Implies force-push. Message stage pre-fills `$EDITOR` with the existing description so typical use is edit-in-place.

With both, the manual dance we just did becomes:
```
vc-x1 push main --amend
```
…which launches `$EDITOR` with the current description pre-filled, goes through review + message approval gates (both critical for a force-push), then squashes + describes + force-pushes both sides atomically.

**2. "Is it worth the effort?"** — Yes, with a caveat on ordering.

**Worth it because**:

- **Recurrence count**: we've done this manual force-push sequence *three times* in one session (tf-1 removal + the squashed 0.37.4 + any future amend). That's not a rare edge case, it's a workflow.
- **Manual steps are where we make mistakes**: the stale-state bug in the 0.37.1 → 0.37.2 flow (push's state file thinking we'd finalized when we'd done it out-of-band) was exactly the kind of coordination failure that a properly-integrated amend stage prevents — because push would own the state and would know "I just rewrote @-, my next stage is force-push-app, not resume-where-I-stopped-last-time."
- **It completes the push design**: today's manual "Late changes after push" flow is a documented escape hatch. If amend lands inside push, the escape hatch retires and everything goes through the same two approval gates.

**Caveats on ordering**:

- The state-sanity + stage-prereq todos from earlier this session (resume on stale state silently skipped work) should land BEFORE amend. Amend is a force-push; we want the state machine rock-solid first.
- Non-fast-forward pushing needs a `--force-with-lease`-equivalent to refuse if someone else pushed in between. Otherwise amend becomes a quiet history-eraser.
- Approval-gate UX: the existing `review` gate shows a diff stat; for amend we should additionally surface "this will rewrite commit X → Y, force-pushing to remote" in the review so the user knows what they're authorizing.

**Recommended ordering**:

1. Ship current 0.37.3 (manual force-push — the last one we'll do manually).
2. State-sanity preflight + stage-prereq checks (the existing todos).
3. `--scope=app|claude|both` (simpler, independent value).
4. `--amend` (the big one, benefits from both of the above being in place).

Want me to capture this as a todo entry so we don't lose the framing, and then back to Step 6 of the current push sequence?

> DEVELOPER

yes please capture, but should we do it now with this inbetween state?

> AGENT

Good question. Three choices:

**A — Edit todo.md now, re-squash into @-, re-describe, force-push.** One clean commit. Cost: another describe cycle, and the body bullet for "notes/todo.md" would need to expand to mention the new entry.

**B — Finish the force-push first, then add the todo entry as a small follow-up commit.** Current step stays atomic. The amend-mode framing lives in a separate tiny commit ("docs: capture amend-mode + single-repo-scope design (0.37.4)"). No force-push for the follow-up since it's a new commit forward.

**C — Capture the framing in this conversation text now (for immediate short-term memory), add to todo.md after push.** Same end state as B but the design notes are visible in-thread until we commit them.

My lean: **B**. The 0.37.3 commit we're about to force-push already has a cohesive story (bug fixes + rename + promote). Adding a design-notes bullet stretches its scope awkwardly — those notes are forward-looking, not retrospective. Also, the less we touch @- before force-push, the less rewriting we're doing against the remote. B gives us a clean force-push, then a natural tiny commit for the design capture, no force-push on that.

Your call though — any of A/B/C works. Which?
